  documented a route around the generated client — the features README's worked example
  hand-typed `IStudentListItem` against a hardcoded `/api/v1/Students`, and the api README
  sanctioned the plain `@/lib/http` verb helpers "for anything not yet worth a typed wrapper".
  Both rewritten so `apiGet`/`apiPost` is the only sanctioned route; the general permission is
  replaced by a specific instruction (add `apiPut`/`apiPatch`/`apiDelete` to `client.ts` first,
  since only GET and POST are wired). The worked example now uses the one path the contract
  actually declares, `/api/v1/reference/records`, with types indexed off
  `components['schemas']` so they cannot drift — and it flags that the `Reference` tag is test
  scaffolding, not a product tag, so it is not mistaken for a real feature folder.
  **S5:** `lint` is `oxlint --max-warnings=0`, making the `suspicious` category and five named
  rules enforceable rather than aspirational. Zero diagnostics surfaced, matching TASK-0001's
  measurement — no mass-fix.
  **S6:** `httpClient` no longer re-exported from `src/lib/http/index.ts`, and
  `http-boundary.test.ts` gained a case failing on a `httpClient` import from outside
  `src/lib/http/` — enforced, not merely tidied.
  **S7:** the catch-all route's element is wrapped in `ErrorBoundary`, making
  `app-router.tsx`'s own doc comment true rather than false.
  **Test count:** HANDOFF.md claimed 120, STATE.md 129; the real number is **130 across 15
  files**, the extra one being this card's own new boundary case. Both corrected.
  **Orchestrator verification** went past the report on one point, because README code is not
  typechecked and this card exists to stop a template teaching something wrong: the rewritten
  example's `apiGet(path, params, options?)` / `apiPost(path, body, options?)` shape matches
  `src/api/client.ts:59,79` exactly, and `SampleRecordDto`, `CreateSampleRecordCommand` and
  `PagedResultOfSampleRecordDto` all exist in `schema.d.ts` (:325, :105, :204). Gates re-run by
  the orchestrator rather than accepted on report, since the agent's paste was reformatted
  rather than verbatim: verify green with 130/130 and 0 skipped, api-drift clean.
  **Accepted limitation, deliberately not widened:** the new boundary regex matches named
  imports only. `http-client.ts` exports `httpClient` as a named export only, so a default or
  namespace import is a form the codebase cannot currently produce. Recorded, not carded.
  S8 (`src/shared/`) untouched — Open question 11 still needs a human call.

## 2026-09-04 — TASK-0013 closure note

- 2026-09-04 **TASK-0013 CLOSED — idempotency deferred with a binding record, not a shrug.**
  Option B, human sign-off the same day: do not build the mechanism, land a record that will
  actually fire. Two files: `backend/docs/ASSUMPTIONS.md` §2.14 (new, marked DEVIATION in the
  §2.2 style) and a `TODO(TASK-0013)` in `ReferenceEndpoints.cs` — the class `<remarks>`, plus a
  `//` note in the `MapCreateSampleRecord` handler body because that method is what gets copied.
  Zero behaviour change; no filter, no middleware, no key store, no package, no migration.
  **The actual deliverable was closing a teaching gap, not a functional one.** `ReferenceEndpoints`
  is the declared copy-from template and its doc comment enumerated "the same five things every
  endpoint below demonstrates" without idempotency; every future POST copied from it inherited the
  omission silently. The record deliberately does NOT add idempotency as a sixth item on that list,
  because it is not yet true of anything in the file — it is a labelled TODO instead, which is the
  honest shape. Owner `backend-dev`; orchestrator accountable for firing the trigger at dispatch.
  Trigger written so it cannot be read narrowly: the first card implementing **any** retry-duplicable
  mutation builds the mechanism first, §9.8.2's four named operations being examples rather than an
  exhaustive list. Drift entry with the full reasoning: `drift/2026-Q3.md`, 2026-09-04 entry.
  **Contract deliberately did not move** — `b287da03…` byte-identical before and after, which was an
  acceptance criterion rather than a nicety: XML doc comments flow into the generated OpenAPI
  document automatically in .NET 10 (`ASSUMPTIONS.md` §5), so a doc-comment TODO placed in an
  operation's `<summary>`/`<remarks>` or in a `.WithDescription(...)` would have silently rewritten
  the contract on a documentation-only card. Placement in the class-level `<remarks>` of an
  `IEndpointModule` and in a handler body avoids it. Verified twice — by `ci.ps1`'s drift gate and
  independently by `contract-guardian` running all four §4.4 checks. **Generalises: on this stack a
  comment can be contract content, so a "documentation-only" backend diff is not automatically
  contract-safe and must still be hash-checked.**
  Gates: all ten green live, `total=180 passed=180 failed=0 skipped=0`, line coverage 75.61%,
  integration suite 30/30 actually run. §4.4 checks 1-4 all PASS, `frontend/src/api/` and
  `contracts/` untouched in `git status`, no `GENERATED` header touched.

## 2026-09-04 — TASK-0015 closure note

- 2026-09-04 **TASK-0015 CLOSED — §6 audit S1-S3 (backend scaffold conformance), Phase 0b now
  complete.**
  **S1** (`/health/ready` anonymous, unthrottled, hits the database): resolved with a dedicated
  `RateLimitingOptions.HealthPolicyName` fixed-window policy, `RequireRateLimiting`'d onto
  `/health/ready` only — `/health/live` stays unthrottled since it runs no dependency checks and
  amplifies nothing. Numbers: `HealthPermitLimit=120`, `HealthWindowSeconds=60` (2 req/s
  sustained), assuming a 5-second probe-interval floor (tightest of the common defaults —
  Kubernetes' own default is 10s, its allowed minimum 1s; AWS ALB/NLB's minimum is 5s; Docker's
  default 30s) with up to 3 independent probers sharing one partition key (a load balancer, an
  orchestrator's kubelet, and an external uptime monitor behind the same NAT/egress). Worst case
  at that floor is 36 req/min; 120 leaves >3x headroom so no legitimate cadence can ever trip it,
  while still capping an anonymous DB-touching endpoint far below "unlimited". Per the 2026-09-04
  card log steer, this is the app-level limiter — no ingress claim made; Open question 5
  (deployment target) stays open and undecided. Raised in Development
  (`appsettings.Development.template.json`) and in the integration test fixture, mirroring the
  existing `PermitLimit` override pattern, to avoid test flakiness.
  **S2** (Api importing `SchoolManagement.Infrastructure.Persistence` inside the HTTP pipeline):
  new Application port `IPersistenceErrorTranslator`
  (`Abstractions/Persistence/IPersistenceErrorTranslator.cs`), implemented by
  `Infrastructure.Persistence.PersistenceErrorTranslator` (a thin adapter delegating to the
  existing static `PersistenceErrors.TryTranslate` — that method is unchanged and stays
  independently unit-testable). Registered as a singleton (stateless; `GlobalExceptionHandler`,
  its only consumer, is itself a framework-registered singleton). `GlobalExceptionHandler` now
  depends on the port, not the Infrastructure type.
  **S3** (no architecture test for Api→Infrastructure): added
  `DependencyDirectionTests.Api_DoesNotDependOnInfrastructureOutsideComposition`. Confirmed
  empirically (build + test, not assumed) that only ONE narrow exemption was needed beyond
  `Program.cs`: `StartupEnvironmentGuard`, an `IHostedService` that inspects Infrastructure's
  `DatabaseOptions` at `StartAsync` — before the host accepts traffic — to refuse an unsafe
  deployed configuration. Both exemptions are startup/composition-time wiring, never
  request-path code, which is the same category the rule exists to keep clean. (A third
  candidate, `HealthEndpoints.cs`'s `using SchoolManagement.Infrastructure;` for
  `InfrastructureDependencyInjection.ReadinessTag`, needed no exemption at all: `ReadinessTag` is
  a C# `const`, so the compiler inlines its value at the call site and the compiled assembly
  carries no metadata reference to `InfrastructureDependencyInjection` — verified by running the
  test before adding any exemption and observing only `StartupEnvironmentGuard` reported.)
  **Deviation from the card's literal wording, flagged for visibility, since ACCEPTED:** the
  2026-09-04 dispatch said "whatever narrow exemption `Program.cs` genuinely needs", anticipating
  the carve-out would be Program.cs alone. The pre-existing `StartupEnvironmentGuard` dependency
  was not named in the card or in AUDIT.md's S2 finding. Extending the exemption to it (rather
  than also porting `DatabaseOptions` behind an abstraction, which is not in the card's acceptance
  criteria) was a backend-dev judgement call to unblock the test without scope creep. **Ruled
  ACCEPTED on reopen (below)** — orchestrator agreed an `IHostedService` reading `DatabaseOptions`
  at `StartAsync` is startup composition, not request-path code, and recorded a `## Known drift`
  entry with a trigger so a third exemption has to argue for itself.
  **S3 (stale artefact) — WIDENED ON REOPEN, see below for the corrected version.** Originally
  landed comparing the artefact's `LastWriteTimeUtc` against the newest `.cs` file under
  `src/SchoolManagement.Api` only, per the card's literal wording at the time.
  **Contract deliberately did not move** — `b287da03…` byte-identical before and after, verified
  both by direct `sha256sum` comparison and by `ci.ps1`'s own drift gate ("The committed contract
  matches the code"). The `/health/*` endpoints are `.ExcludeFromDescription()`'d and the new/
  changed doc comments (`HealthEndpoints.cs`, `RateLimitingOptions.cs`) sit on types that never
  reach the document, unlike TASK-0013's lesson about endpoint-level XML comments.
  Gates (initial close): all ten green live via `scripts/local-env.ps1` (DB resolved from
  `$HOME/.gras/pg-test.txt`), `total=181 passed=181 failed=0 skipped=0`, line coverage 75.86% /
  branch 57.60% (60% floor; the 87.00%/77.18% attribution question is TASK-0002/TASK-0009
  territory and was not touched). Diff: 10 files changed + 2 new
  (`IPersistenceErrorTranslator.cs`, `PersistenceErrorTranslator.cs`), 171 insertions / 6
  deletions — under the ~400-line guidance. Out-of-scope items (TASK-0012/0013 territory, the
  coverage number, the recorded nice-to-haves) left untouched, as instructed.

  **REOPENED same day — the staleness check was narrower than the defect it targets.** The
  Api-only scan was a faithful implementation of the card's own wording, but the card's wording
  was itself too narrow: the OpenAPI document is also shaped by types in
  `SchoolManagement.Application` — `ReferenceEndpoints.cs` returns `PingResponse`,
  `PagedResult<SampleRecordDto>` and `CreateSampleRecordResponse`, all declared there — so editing
  one of those changed the document while the Api-only check stayed green. That is the "stale
  passes silently" case the criterion exists to close, still open on a live path. Narrowing
  attributed to the card, not to the implementation; card corrected.
  **Fix:** `FindNewestApiSourceFile` renamed to `FindNewestBackendSourceFile` and widened from
  `src/SchoolManagement.Api` to all of `backend/src` (all four projects) — same `bin`/`obj`
  exclusion, same relative-path-and-both-timestamps failure message, same null-safe behaviour when
  the directory is absent. `OpenApiDocument`'s class `<remarks>` and the method doc comment
  rewritten to match: the old "that project is what generate-openapi.ps1 starts" justification is
  gone, replaced with the cross-project example above plus a new paragraph recording the accepted
  trade-off — a wider scan is slightly more likely to hit filesystem timestamp jitter on a fresh
  checkout, accepted because it fails loudly and by name rather than passing silently, and cannot
  fire at all inside `ci.ps1`.
  **Verified empirically:** touched
  `src/SchoolManagement.Application/Reference/Ping/PingQuery.cs`, confirmed the architecture tests
  failed with the message naming that exact file and timestamp, regenerated, confirmed all 27
  architecture tests passed again. Contract hash reconfirmed `b287da03…` byte-identical.
  Gates (refix): all ten green live via `scripts/local-env.ps1`, `total=181 passed=181 failed=0
  skipped=0`, line coverage 75.86% / branch 57.60%. Nothing else in the accepted diff touched.

  **REOPENED AGAIN same day, narrowly — criterion 1 (the health rate limit) landed with no test,
  and a pre-existing fixture comment made a false coverage claim.** `.RequireRateLimiting
  (RateLimitingOptions.HealthPolicyName)` in `HealthEndpoints.cs` was the one new production
  behaviour this card introduced and nothing asserted it — deleting the line left the whole suite
  green, which is exactly the "documented rather than enforced" defect family this card exists to
  eliminate. Separately, `ApiTestFixture.cs:124-125` said "the rate limiter itself is verified by a
  dedicated test that sets its own limit" — a grep for `429`/`TooManyRequests`/
  `Status429TooManyRequests` outside `bin`/`obj` found no such test anywhere in the backend. Both
  pre-existing, not introduced by the S1 implementation; worse than a plain gap because the comment
  tells the next reader to stop looking (same family as TASK-0016's fixtures, TASK-0010's document
  target).
  **Fix — three items, nothing else:**
  (1) New `tests/SchoolManagement.IntegrationTests/HealthRateLimitTests.cs`:
  `Ready_IsRejectedOnceTheHealthPolicyLimitIsExceeded` builds its own client via
  `Fixture.WithWebHostBuilder(...)`, layering `HealthPermitLimit=2` on the shared fixture's
  already-booted database connection (no new container, no new migration run — avoids driving
  10000+ real round trips against the shared Neon instance to reach the raised shared limit),
  sends three requests to `/health/ready`, asserts the third is `429`.
  (2) `Live_StaysUnthrottled_EvenUnderAHealthPolicyTightEnoughToRejectReady` — same technique,
  `HealthPermitLimit=1`, five requests to `/health/live`, all asserted `200` — cheap enough (one
  more `[Fact]`, same file) to include per the card's "if it costs only a few lines" steer.
  (3) `ApiTestFixture.cs:124-125` comment rewritten to name what is actually verified
  (`HealthRateLimitTests`, health policy only) and state plainly that the default and sensitive
  policies have no coverage — that gap is recorded as drift separately, TASK-0003 as the trigger
  (login is the sensitive policy's first real user).
  **Verified the new test can actually fail, not just pass — same standard as the prior reopen:**
  commented out `.RequireRateLimiting(RateLimitingOptions.HealthPolicyName)`, rebuilt, reran —
  `Ready_...` failed with `third.StatusCode should be TooManyRequests but was OK`; `Live_...` still
  passed (correctly — it was never gated on that line). Restored the line, rebuilt, reran — both
  passed; `git diff --stat` on `HealthEndpoints.cs` matches the prior round's diff exactly (17
  lines), confirming no residue from the round-trip.
  **Out of scope honoured:** no test for the default or sensitive policies; no other fixture
  refactor.
  Contract reconfirmed `b287da03…` byte-identical. Gates all ten green live,
  `total=183 passed=183 failed=0 skipped=0` (181 + 2 new), line coverage 75.16% / branch 57.60%.

## 2026-09-05 — Open question 11 RESOLVED by human decision: follow §7

**The question**, open since TASK-0001 (2026-08-27): the frontend scaffold deviates from §7 in three
places, all called deliberate in `frontend/HANDOFF.md` — a document written 2026-08-03, **before the
product specification landed**, so the deviations were chosen without knowing what would be built.
(a) §7 puts screens under `src/features/<feature>/`; the scaffold has a top-level `src/screens/` and
no `src/shared/`. (b) `react-hook-form` plus a zod resolver is mandated and absent. (c) Playwright is
mandated for auth, the primary create path and one failure path, and is absent. Each becomes binding
the moment a screen, a form or a flow exists — i.e. at TASK-0003's frontend half.

**Decision (human, 2026-09-05): follow §7's mandates, with the proviso "as long as it will lead to a
better file and codebase structure."** All three deviations are retired. `HANDOFF.md`'s reasoning is
superseded, not overruled on its own terms: it was correct for a design-system-first scaffold with no
product spec, and stopped being correct when the spec landed.

**How the proviso is applied.** It is a guard against churn, not a licence to re-open the decision per
case. Read as: adopt the §7 structure for everything new, and move existing code only where the move
buys something. Concretely —
- `src/features/<feature>/` is the home for every new screen. `src/features/` is currently empty and
  reserved, so TASK-0003's screens land there with nothing to migrate.
- `src/shared/` is created when the first genuinely shared thing needs it, and existing
  `src/components/`, `src/lib/`, `src/stores/` are NOT bulk-renamed into it on their own. A rename of
  a working, 130-test scaffold buys structure on paper and costs a large reviewable diff — that is
  exactly what the proviso excludes. The one thing that must not happen is a THIRD home for shared
  code appearing alongside them.
- `src/screens/scaffold-status/` is deleted when the first real screen lands, per its own drift entry,
  which removes `src/screens/` rather than migrating it.
- `react-hook-form` + `@hookform/resolvers` (zod) are added at the first form — TASK-0003's login.
  Dependency additions justified in that card per §8: spec-mandated by §7.
- Playwright is added at the first flow — TASK-0003's auth flow is the exact case §7 names first.

**Consequences.** TASK-0003's frontend half is now larger than a §4.3 "adapt callers" step: it
introduces a directory convention, two dependencies, and an E2E harness alongside the first real
screen. It splits. The `src/shared/` drift entry (2026-08-27) is resolved; the `src/screens/`
question inside it is answered.

**What this does NOT decide:** the bearer-token auth in `frontend/src/lib/auth/` is still known-wrong
against the cookie sign-off and is still TASK-0003's to fix — that was never part of question 11.

## 2026-09-05 — approved contract deltas moved to their own archive

Contract deltas are a distinct artefact class from decisions and will recur on every card that
touches the boundary. Appending 30 KB of endpoint shapes into this file per card would make it
unreadable, so approved deltas now live in `.agent/decisions/2026-Q3-contract-deltas.md`, one
entry per card, indexed from the card itself. First entry: TASK-0003.

## 2026-09-05 — TASK-0003 closure note (full text, condensed in STATE.md on 2026-09-05)

**TASK-0003 CLOSED.** Six `/api/v1/auth/*` endpoints, contract `3b518bd9…`, 245/245 gates green.
BREAKING only in removing `/reference/whoami` (zero callers). Rulings embedded in the
implementation: Super Admin is a flag bypass (spec 6.1.7 over 4.2.2); `423` is returned only when
the password is correct, so a locked account does not leak its own existence to a wrong guess.

**Reopened four times, always the same shape: the feature worked and its test passed while the
RULE went unenforced.** Landed as one ~1443-line diff instead of the three seams the card
envisaged. The second-pass review that size bought found three HIGH defects that 237 passing
tests were blind to. The lesson is not "write more tests" — it is that a test asserting the
feature does what it does cannot detect that the feature is not doing what the rule requires.

## 2026-09-05 — TASK-0022 closure note

**Discovered by raising PR #9**, the first time `backend-ci` ran against the branch. Job
`quality-gates` failed at "Self-test the gate-summary trx parser" in 7s, cascading into two
further errors that were symptoms, not causes.

**Three stacked defects, each hidden by the one in front of it:**

1. `scripts/tests/gate-summary.tests.ps1:79` invoked `& powershell.exe`. CI runs the step with
   `shell: pwsh` on `ubuntu-latest`, where that binary does not exist; `$ErrorActionPreference =
   'Stop'` turned it into a terminating error. Fixed by resolving `(Get-Process -Id $PID).Path` —
   the host the suite is itself running under. `$IsWindows`/`$IsCoreCLR` are unusable here: they
   are undefined in Windows PowerShell 5.1 and `Set-StrictMode -Version Latest` throws on an
   undefined variable. The same hardcode was fixed in `local-env.tests.ps1:107` (latent — that
   suite is not wired into any workflow).
2. `scripts/lib/gate-summary.ps1:80` derived the suite name via
   `[System.IO.Path]::GetFileNameWithoutExtension($storage)`, and every fixture carried a Windows
   `storage` path. On Unix, .NET recognises only `/` as a separator — a backslash is an ordinary
   filename character — so this would have returned the entire mangled path once defect 1 was
   fixed. Now splits on `/` and `\` explicitly. A new `skipped-unix/` fixture carries a real
   `/home/runner/...` path, and an assertion pins both shapes to the same derived suite name.
3. **`backend/.gitignore:18` (`*.trx`) meant NO fixture had ever been committed** — not
   `skipped-unix/`, and not the pre-existing `happy/`, `failed/` or `skipped/`. `git ls-files`
   returned only `fake-gate.ps1`. On a fresh `actions/checkout` those four directories do not
   exist, so **this self-test has never been capable of passing in CI**; defect 1 merely surfaced
   first and masked it. Fixed with a scoped negation, `!scripts/tests/fixtures/**/*.trx`, chosen
   over `git add -f` so future fixtures are included automatically and the intent is documented
   where the exclusion lives.

**Verification (performed by the orchestrator, not the subagent — see the dispatch note below).**
Mutation test: `Get-SuiteName` reverted to pre-fix behaviour → 3 of 25 assertions failed, exit 1;
restored → byte-exact against the index, 25/25, exit 0. `.gitignore` scoping proved by cases
rather than by reading — `artifacts/coverage/*.trx`, `TestResults/*.trx` and
`tests/**/TestResults/*.trx` all still ignored, fixtures re-included. Full gates: ten PASS,
`total=245 passed=245 failed=0 skipped=0`, line 77.93% / branch 59.08%, contract drift gate green
and the hash unmoved.

**Weakness found BY the mutation run, and worth acting on.** Under the mutant, the two
process-mode assertions at `gate-summary.tests.ps1:101` and `:108` still PASSED: they use
`-match 'schoolmanagement\.integrationtests'`, which matches as a substring of the fully mangled
path. Only the three in-process `-eq` assertions caught the break. Those two read as though they
verify correct suite derivation and would not fail if it were wrong. This is the standing
2026-09-04 lesson recurring inside the very suite that exists to enforce it: a check that cannot
be shown to fail is not a check, and a substring match on a long string is close to unfalsifiable.

**Dispatch note — a process failure worth recording.** The `backend-dev` dispatch stalled three
consecutive times, each returning a "waiting for…" string in place of the report its card
required, and it missed defect 3 entirely. The orchestrator caught defect 3 in review, and
performed the breakage proof and the gate run itself. Two lessons: the §9 rule that a subagent
reporting success without pasting gate output has not finished did its job — there was no output
to accept, so nothing was accepted; and reviewing the DIFF rather than the REPORT is what found
the untracked fixtures, since no report existed to be misled by.

**The through-line.** Third instance of the TASK-0011 pattern — a gate that had never actually
executed was reporting PASS. This one lived in CI for a week without running, and only raising a
PR revealed it. The gates were well written; the failure was again in whether they ran at all.

## 2026-09-05 — TASK-0023, 0024, 0025 closure notes, and the standing lesson they share

All three came out of PR #9, the first time either workflow ran against this branch. Read together
they are one finding, which is why the standing lesson is recorded here rather than three times.

### The standing lesson — inputs that live outside the repository

**Four defects in one day, all the same shape: something required to run the gates was not in the
repository, so "green locally" and "green in CI" were never the same claim.**

1. TASK-0022 — the gate self-test's `.trx` fixtures were `*.trx`-ignored and had never been
   committed. The suite could not run on a fresh checkout.
2. TASK-0023 — `frontend/.env` is gitignored, and `env-values.ts` validates at module scope, so
   all 15 test files died during collection on a runner. The suite had never run in CI.
3. TASK-0024 (a) — `scripts/local-env.ps1` is untracked and per-developer; this machine's copy
   predates TASK-0018 and still carries the bug that card fixed in the template.
4. TASK-0024 (b) — two tool VERSIONS were unpinned in opposite directions: `global.json`'s
   `rollForward: latestFeature` let CI take a newer SDK feature band than any developer had, and
   CI pinned gitleaks 8.28.0 while the developer ran 8.30.1.

**The rule that follows:** a gate is only as trustworthy as the reproducibility of its inputs.
Before trusting a green gate, ask what it reads that is not committed and not version-pinned.
`frontend-ci.yml` already had the right instinct — it pins Node "so CI cannot pass on a runtime
nobody runs locally" — and the backend violated exactly that principle in both directions.

Corollary on measurement: the gitleaks split was *measured*, not deduced — same repo, same 21
commits, same `.gitleaks.toml`, 8.30.1 reports `no leaks found` and 8.28.0 reports 15. Two tool
versions disagreeing about whether a repository contains secrets is not a difference of opinion;
it means the gate's verdict depended on which machine ran it.

### TASK-0023 — frontend test environment

`vite.config.ts`'s `test` block now declares an explicit `env` with all six `VITE_*` variables, so
vitest depends on no ambient file. Proved by moving `frontend/.env` aside: 15/15 files, 130/130
tests green with `Test-Path .env = False`. The case asserting "loads the real .env without
throwing" was the defect stated as a test and was reframed, not deleted; its real assertions stand.

Related gap CONFIRMED and deliberately not fixed: `vite build` does not execute `env-values.ts` —
it inlines `import.meta.env.VITE_*` as `undefined` — so with no `.env` the build SUCCEEDS while
emitting a bundle that throws on boot. The Build gate goes green on something unusable. The
workflow now copies `.env.example` (explicitly non-secret) for build/preview/E2E; the unit tests
deliberately do not rely on that copy. **A build-time env check is unowned and uncarded.**

### TASK-0024 — backend toolchain pinning

`rollForward` `latestFeature` → `latestPatch`: feature bands, not patches, carry new analyzer
versions, so this pins CI and local to the same analyzer set by construction while still
tolerating a security patch within the band. CI's gitleaks bumped 8.28.0 → 8.30.1 to match local,
and a `dotnet --version` step now prints the resolved SDK so the next divergence is read rather
than deduced.

Both analyzer findings were FIXED, not suppressed, and one was a genuine bug:

- **CA2025** (`PrivilegeAuthorizationTests.SendAsAsync`) — a non-async method held
  `using var request = new HttpRequestMessage(...)` and returned `_client.SendAsync(request, …)`'s
  Task directly. `Dispose()` therefore ran the moment `SendAsync` RETURNED ITS TASK, not when the
  send completed, so `HttpClient` could be reading a disposed request. Made `async`/`await`ed.
- **CA1873** (`RequestLoggingBehavior`, cancellation branch) — the `[LoggerMessage]` source
  generator only skips INTERNAL formatting when the level is disabled; a computed argument at the
  call site is still evaluated unconditionally. Guarded with `IsEnabled`.

Honest limits of the verification, recorded because they matter: this machine has only SDK
10.0.100, so the newer feature band that CI had drifted to could not be installed. The pin is
verified by SDK-resolution semantics, NOT by reproducing the failures and watching them disappear
— which is a weaker standard than this project's own "break it and watch it go red". The
`dotnet test` "argument <dll> is invalid" error likewise could not be reproduced locally.

### TASK-0025 — the client regeneration TASK-0003 skipped

§4.3's handoff is backend promotes → frontend regenerates. TASK-0003 promoted `3b518bd9…` and
closed with the second half never run, and **nothing recorded it** until 2026-09-05. The committed
`schema.d.ts` still declared `/reference/whoami`, removed as a BREAKING change, and none of the six
`/auth/*` paths. `check:api-drift` was correctly failing the whole time.

**The process defect is not the stale file; it is that a half-finished §4.3 handoff could close a
card.** §10's checklist covers "contract regenerated and lockfile current" — which was true on the
backend side — without asking whether the CONSUMER caught up.

## 2026-09-05 — TASK-0026: analyzer/test-bridge behaviour made independent of the installed SDK

TASK-0024's rollForward pin was verified by SDK-resolution semantics, not reproduction, and CI's
next run disproved it: CA1873 fired at RequestLoggingBehavior.cs(84,21) INSIDE the guard TASK-0024
added, and dotnet test failed with "The argument dll is invalid." Neither reproduced locally on SDK
10.0.100 at the time the card was written. This entry records what actually reproduced once
investigated properly, and what still did not.

### Root cause 1: AnalysisLevel=latest-All is a property of the exact SDK BUILD, not the feature band

Microsoft.NET.Sdk.Analyzers.targets (inside the SDK install) hard-codes an internal constant
(EffectiveAnalysisLevel's source) per SDK build; its own comment says the value is bumped as the
platform matures. VERIFIED directly: dotnet build -getProperty:EffectiveAnalysisLevel with
AnalysisLevel=latest-All on this machine's SDK 10.0.100 resolves to 9.0, not 10.0, even though the
TFM is net10.0. Forcing -p:AnalysisLevel=10.0-All on this SAME SDK reproduced BOTH CI failures
locally: CA1873 at RequestLoggingBehavior.cs(84,21) (matching the card's cited location exactly)
AND a second, previously-unreported instance at RequestLoggingBehavior.cs(60,21) (the
RequestFailed call, unguarded). This is the local repro the card asked for: no second SDK was
needed, forcing the property on the SAME installed SDK was sufficient because the rule SET (not
just the rule CODE) is gated by this value.

Fix: AnalysisLevel pinned to 10.0-All (Directory.Build.props) plus an explicit
Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.NetAnalyzers PackageVersion (10.0.100, Directory.Packages.props) to pin the
rule CODE too, since the SDK-bundled analyzer assembly can also change within a feature band. A
bare PackageReference to that package does NOT supersede the SDK's bundled copy on this SDK:
VERIFIED via dotnet build -v:diag, grepping the literal csc.exe analyzer switches on the command
line — only the SDK-install paths appeared, with or without the bare reference, because the pinned
package (10.0.100) ships no build/props/targets asset of its own, so nothing wires it in.
backend/Directory.Build.targets (new file) removes the SDK's implicit Analyzer items by metadata
match and re-adds the pinned package's DLLs, inside a Target with BeforeTargets CoreCompile (a
static ItemGroup outside a Target cannot batch-Remove by item metadata; MSBuild raises MSB4191).
Re-verified via the same analyzer-switch grep after the fix: SDK paths gone, pinned paths present.

Four total CA1873 sites needed the guard-and-hoist-to-a-local fix (the pattern TASK-0024 already
used for the success path): RequestLoggingBehavior.cs lines 56-60 (RequestFailed, newly surfaced)
and 79-85 (RequestCancelled, the guard alone was insufficient because BehaviorLog.RequestCancelled
is a static LoggerMessage method taking logger as a parameter, so the analyzer cannot tie
logger.IsEnabled to it without a hoisted local); plus three sites in GlobalExceptionHandler.cs
(RequestAborted, RejectedMalformedRequest, UnhandledException, all passing httpContext.Request.Path,
a PathString whose implicit conversion to string is the flagged expensive evaluation) and one in
EndpointModuleExtensions.cs (RegisteredEndpointModules, a string.Join over reflected type names).
None of these six were in the card's literal text; all reproduce and clear only once AnalysisLevel
is genuinely 10.0, so leaving them unfixed would have reopened the gate the moment the pin took
effect for real.

Roslynator's analyzers (already referenced pre-TASK-0026) were separately confirmed, via the same
analyzer-switch grep, to be genuinely loaded and running (their roslynX.Y-versioned DLL paths
appear on the compile line) — ruled out as a concern, not a new finding.

### Root cause 2: the test-runner bridge was inherited from a transitive package default, not declared

xunit.v3 (Microsoft.Testing.Platform-native) and xunit.runner.visualstudio (the classic VSTest
adapter bridge) are both referenced; which one dotnet test actually invokes is decided by MSBuild
property TestingPlatformDotnetTestSupport, whose DEFAULT is defined inside the
Microsoft.Testing.Platform.MSBuild package (a transitive dependency of xunit.v3 via
xunit.v3.core.mtp-v1, pinned only as a floor, version 1.9.1, not an exact version, and this repo
has no NuGet lock file anywhere). At that floor version the default is false (classic VSTest
bridge), matching what this machine actually runs and what ci.ps1's arguments (settings,
logger trx, results-directory) assume — VERIFIED by running dotnet test with exactly those
arguments and observing the classic VSTest console output (Results File ...trx, Attachments
...coverage.cobertura.xml). CI's "argument dll is invalid" error is consistent with a newer
Microsoft.Testing.Platform.MSBuild resolving on CI with a flipped default, switching dotnet test to
MTP-native mode, which does not understand VSTest switches — plausible, not reproduced (this
machine cannot install a different SDK to force a different transitive resolution).

Fix: TestingPlatformDotnetTestSupport set to false explicitly in backend/tests/Directory.Build.props.
Attempted, then REVERTED: pinning Microsoft.Testing.Platform.MSBuild's own version via Central
Package Management (mirroring the existing Microsoft.OpenApi CVE-pin pattern) to close the floor-
not-exact gap at the package-version layer too. This broke restore on this machine (NETSDK1013,
empty TargetFramework) because promoting the package from implicit-transitive to centrally-pinned
makes its buildMultiTargeting assets import unconditionally, including into the SDK's outer
cross-targeting evaluation pass (which always runs with an empty TargetFramework, even for a
single-TFM project). Left floating; recorded as new drift below. Trx output and coverlet coverage
both confirmed still working post-fix (ci.ps1's own run: Tests total 245 passed 245 failed 0
skipped 0).

### A genuinely unrelated bug found and fixed along the way

While isolating the restore failure above by testing files in combination, a real, separate defect
surfaced: an XML comment containing a literal double-hyphen sequence (writing CLI flags like
settings/logger-trx as literal double-dash text inside a comment block) corrupts MSBuild's XML
parse in a way that silently propagates a broken, empty TargetFramework through evaluation,
producing exactly the same "Invalid framework identifier" and NETSDK1013 errors as the
Microsoft.Testing.Platform.MSBuild pin above — a red herring that cost significant time before
being isolated by diffing against a freshly-stashed pristine checkout. All double-hyphen sequences
were removed from every comment written for this card. No repo rule currently catches this; not
carded, since it is a self-inflicted authoring hazard rather than a product or CI defect — noted
here so the next agent recognizes the symptom instantly instead of re-discovering it.

### Verified / unverified summary

1. CA1873 fix — VERIFIED: reproduced locally (forcing the real AnalysisLevel value), fixed,
   re-verified clean at that same forced level, then verified again under the actual committed pin
   (full solution build, 0 warnings 0 errors) and under ci.ps1's own Build gate.
2. Analyzer version pin — VERIFIED that the SDK-bundled analyzer is excluded and the pinned one is
   loaded (analyzer-switch command-line grep, before and after). NOT verified: that CI's resolved
   SDK patch would have produced a numerically different EffectiveAnalysisLevel without this fix —
   inferred from the targets file's own hard-coded, per-build constant, not reproduced against a
   second SDK (none available on this machine).
3. Test-bridge property — VERIFIED: dotnet test with ci.ps1's exact arguments produces classic
   VSTest output and a valid trx both before and after the property was made explicit (this
   machine already defaulted to false). NOT verified: that leaving it unset would have actually
   failed on CI's SDK — the property's default is read from a package version this machine cannot
   force to something else without breaking restore (see above).
4. Gate output: ci.ps1 -Configuration Release -NoFailFast — ALL GATES PASSED, 245 of 245 tests,
   0 skipped, line coverage 77.85 percent. Contract hash unchanged (3b518bd9...).

## 2026-09-06 — Open question 12 RESOLVED by human sign-off: TASK-0027 approved as drafted

**The question**, open since the TASK-0019 delta was split on 2026-09-06: CLAUDE.md §5 treats every
session/auth change as requiring human sign-off, and TASK-0027 contains two operations that act on
another account's sessions and credentials — `POST /admins/{id}/password-reset` (mints a temporary
password, sets `must_change_password`, revokes every session the target holds) and
`DELETE /admins/{id}/sessions`. Suspension and deactivation revoke the target's sessions too, so the
sign-off was not avoidable by deferring the two obvious endpoints. The auth MECHANISM was never in
question — it is TASK-0003's cookie session and its existing revocation path, unmoved. What needed a
ruling was the authority: who may operate that machinery on someone else's account.

**Four options were put to the human:** (1) approve as drafted — the privilege grant is the whole
gate; (2) approve with a step-up re-authentication requirement on the two endpoints, reusing the
re-authentication convention TASK-0003 already built; (3) approve but require `is_super_admin` in
addition to the privilege on those two endpoints, narrower than the spec's privilege model and
recordable only as an added assumption; (4) defer the two endpoints to a later card, which would
have shrunk the surface without removing the sign-off.

**Decision (human, 2026-09-06): option 1, approve as drafted.** An account holding
`admin.password.reset` or `admin.session.revoke` may exercise it against another account, with no
step-up re-authentication of the acting admin and no additional `is_super_admin` requirement on
those two endpoints. The spec's privilege model stands unmodified.

**What still guards the operation** — unchanged by this ruling, and the implementing agent must not
treat the sign-off as licence to relax any of it: the at-least-one-active-Super-Admin invariant (4.1)
enforced transactionally under a row lock; the self-status-change block (delta amendment B5, recorded
in `backend/docs/ASSUMPTIONS.md` as an added rule rather than a spec derivation); reactivation
requiring `admin.deactivate` held by a Super Admin (6.1.10); and 6.1.7 rule 4, `is_super_admin`
settable only by an account that already holds it, with an audit event on the rejected attempt.

**No contract shape changed.** The ruling is on authority, not on the wire — the delta ships exactly
as amended, so nothing in it was re-opened or re-drafted. TASK-0027 moved from `blocked` to
`in-progress` and was dispatched to `backend-dev` the same day.


## 2026-09-14 — full text of the 2026-09-05..09-10 `## Decisions` entries, condensed to a one-line index in STATE.md

Moved verbatim from `.agent/STATE.md` (187 KB, 53 entries) during the context-budget pass.
STATE.md now carries the index; this is the text. Nothing was edited on the way across.


- 2026-09-10 **TASK-0054 CLOSED by orchestrator.** Audit-log client seam regenerated against
  `7a3c84e6…`, consuming TASK-0049's and TASK-0055's moves in one pass. §4.4 check 2 RED→GREEN.
  Gates re-run independently: `check:api-drift` "No drift.", **49 files / 350 tests / Skipped 0**,
  build and lint green, gitleaks clean. Two new test files (125 and 80 lines, both under the cap).

  **The eight-run streak of zero client-seam lines ENDED here, correctly.** `SuccessBody` resolved
  `never` for `ExportAuditEvents`: the old `JsonOf` helper matched only an `application/json`
  content key, and this is the contract's first non-JSON response body (`text/csv`). The agent
  proved it with a throwaway type probe BEFORE writing transport code — `error TS2322: ... is not
  assignable to type 'never'` — exactly the "report the failing output, then keep the addition as
  narrow as the gap" discipline the card asked for. Fix is one type-level helper, `ResponseBodyOf`,
  in `client-types.ts`; `client.ts` untouched.

  **How the shared-file risk was discharged — the reusable part.** Green tests cannot prove a type
  *widening* is safe: nothing breaks when a type gets looser. So rather than trusting the gates,
  the orchestrator enumerated every 2xx response across all 47 paths lacking `application/json`:
  **exactly one, `GET /api/v1/audit-events/export`.** Since `ResponseBodyOf` matches
  `application/json` first and falls through only when it is absent, it is byte-identical to
  `JsonOf` for every other operation, and no other operation can even reach the new branch. That
  is a proof about reachability, not an absence of counter-evidence. **Reuse this shape whenever a
  shared type-level helper changes: enumerate what can reach the new branch; do not rely on a
  green suite.**

  **Negative-typing proofs, all honest.** The list/export parameter asymmetry (nine params vs
  seven — no `cursor`/`pageSize` on the export) proven in both directions. For the
  omitted-required-header and omitted-required-path-parameter directions the agent **stated plainly
  that this move offers no new instance** rather than manufacturing one; both operations are
  header-free reads on a path with no `{id}`. Third card running where that instruction produced
  the right behaviour — and it was the honest "no instance here" record on TASK-0054's first draft
  that made the genuine asymmetry instance findable when the facts were re-derived for TASK-0055.

  **One raised risk investigated and closed WITHOUT a card:** `unwrap()` in `src/lib/http/` maps
  any empty response body to `undefined` regardless of content-type, which would mistype an empty
  CSV as `undefined` where the type says `string`. Mechanism real, but unreachable —
  `AuditEventCsvWriter` writes the 13-column header unconditionally before iterating rows, so a
  zero-row export returns the header, never an empty body. Reachable only if that header is ever
  made conditional; recorded on TASK-0054 so the connection stays findable. The agent worked around
  it in its own test rather than reaching into `src/lib/http/` outside its scope — right call.

- 2026-09-10 **TASK-0054 implemented by frontend-dev — client regenerated against `7a3c84e6…`**
  (both TASK-0049's audit read surface and TASK-0055's `entityId` filter, consumed in one pass).
  Status table and closure left to the orchestrator per this dispatch's instructions.

  before committing) assigning a string literal to `SuccessBody<ExportAuditEventsOp>`: `error
  TS2322: Type '"..."' is not assignable to type 'never'.` A real mistype, not a hypothetical one —
  axios does not JSON-parse a `text/csv` body, so the payload exists and is a string at runtime;
  the type just couldn't say so. Fixed with a new `ResponseBodyOf<Content>` helper, narrower than a
  general "any content-type" widening: it only changes the two `SuccessBody` response branches,
  falling through to a response's single content-type value when it isn't `application/json`.
  `RequestBodyOf` (request bodies) still calls the original `JsonOf` unchanged — every request body
  in this contract remains JSON, so widening that side had no proof behind it. Re-ran the same

  body type is untouched. `client.ts` itself needed nothing — both operations reach through the
  existing `apiGet`, confirmed byte-identical (`git diff` on `client.ts` is empty).

  **List/export parameter asymmetry proven in both directions**, per the card's own instruction not
  to fabricate a proof where none exists: `client-audit-events.test.ts` typechecks `ListAuditEvents`

  stated plainly in both test files' header comments as offering no new instance of the

  **The inline-array/no-example MSW trap, ninth confirmation** — `CursorPageOfAuditEventDto` has an

  `text/csv` response has no `application/json` key at all, so `exampleFor` in

  `unwrap()` treats `response.data === ''` as equivalent to `204 No Content` and returns

  Two new test files, both under CONVENTIONS.md §3's 180-line cap: `client-audit-events.test.ts`
  (125 lines — `ListAuditEvents`: nine-params-optional, all-nine-together typecheck, unknown-key
  rejection, `AuditOutcome` §8 tolerance, null `beforeJson`/`afterJson`/`sourceIp` passthrough) and
  `client-audit-events-export.test.ts` (80 lines — `ExportAuditEvents`: string body returned intact,

  (tests table, zero-new-code precedent list, and a new paragraph on the one case that needed code).

  All four `@ts-expect-error`s verified load-bearing by the TASK-0044 removal-probe method (both
  files backed up, all four comment lines deleted at once, `npm run typecheck` reproduced exactly
  four errors at exactly the four expected lines, then restored and re-confirmed clean).

  **Gates**: `npm run typecheck` 0 errors; `npm run lint` 0 warnings; `npm run test` **49 files, 350
  passed, 0 failed, Skipped: 0** (was 47/340 after TASK-0052, +2 files/+10 tests matches exactly);

  **Files changed**: `frontend/src/api/schema.d.ts` (regenerated, +308/-0),
  `frontend/src/api/client-types.ts` (+24/-5 per `git diff --numstat` — the `ResponseBodyOf`

  **Confirmed out of scope, not built, exactly as the card scoped**: no audit-log screen, no filter

- 2026-09-09 **TASK-0055 CLOSED by orchestrator, first-run pass.** `entityId` filter added to both
  audit operations, resolving open question 14 the way the human directed. Contract
  **`7a3c84e6…`** — **47 paths and 86 schemas, both sets diffed key-for-key against HEAD as
  IDENTICAL**, so the move added no path and no schema, only one optional query parameter on two
  existing operations. That set-identity diff is a stronger additive proof than a line-count diff
  and is worth reusing for any parameter-only move. Gate ALL TEN GREEN: `total=906 passed=906
  failed=0 skipped=0`, coverage line=81.51%. ~26 production lines across 7 files.

  **The design detail that matters:** the filter is implemented once, in the shared
  `AuditEventQueryRepository.ApplyFilters`, which both `ListAsync` and `StreamAsync` call. The card
  warned that "the export honouring a filter the list ignores" was the bug to avoid; with a single
  filter implementation that class of bug is structurally impossible rather than merely tested
  against. `BuildFilterMetadata` now writes seven keys, which is what finally makes TASK-0049's
  "exporting one pupil's rows must be distinguishable after the fact" criterion fully satisfiable —
  it was only half-satisfiable while no per-entity filter existed.

  **Sequencing decision, vindicated:** this card was deliberately put AHEAD of TASK-0054 rather
  than after it, so the frontend client is regenerated once against a contract already carrying all
  nine parameters. Running TASK-0054 first would have made its output dead within the hour — the
  same reasoning that widened TASK-0052 to consume two moves in one pass.

  **Side benefit found while re-deriving TASK-0054's facts with `jq`:** the two operations carry
  deliberately DIFFERENT parameter sets — the list nine, the export seven, with no `cursor`/
  `pageSize` on the export because it streams the whole filtered set. TASK-0054 previously had to
  state that this contract move offered no negative-typing instance; that asymmetry is a real one,
  and is now an acceptance criterion there. Worth noting that the honest "no instance available,
  do not fabricate one" record is precisely what made the genuine instance findable later.

- 2026-09-09 **TASK-0049 CLOSED by orchestrator after one reopen.** Audit log read surface:
  `GET /api/v1/audit-events` (cursor-paged, eight optional filters) and
  `GET /api/v1/audit-events/export` (`text/csv`, streamed, self-auditing). Contract
  **`a1bd936b…`, 47 paths**, additive. Final gate **ALL TEN GREEN: total=903 passed=903 failed=0
  skipped=0**, coverage line=81.50% branch=69.35%. ~780 production lines against a ~900 budget.

  **Reviewed sound against §6:** cursor keyed on the composite `(occurred_at DESC, id DESC)` with a
  `0x1F` separator neither field can produce and a `TryDecode` that returns false rather than
  throwing on a tampered value; the tie-break test seeds five rows at one identical instant and
  walks three pages at `pageSize=2` asserting the exact sequence (a distinct-timestamp test would
  never catch a skip); the filter test uses three near-miss rows each matching exactly two of three
  filters, so it proves intersection rather than merely that each filter runs; the export is
  modelled as an `ICommand` so `UnitOfWorkBehavior` commits the self-log row before the caller can
  touch the stream, making "an export that does not log itself fails this card" true by
  construction rather than by convention; filter metadata writes every key even when null, so an
  unfiltered and a narrowed export are distinguishable; CSV is 13 columns with no widening and
  `source_ip` passed through as stored.

  **REOPENED once, for a contract hole the gate could not catch.** The export's 200 declared no
  `content` — no `text/csv`, no schema — while the same operation's 401/403/422/429 and
  `ListAuditEvents`' own 200 all carried content. Runtime was correct and tested; only the document
  was silent. Reopened rather than carded because §3 makes `openapi.json` the single source of
  truth for every byte crossing the boundary and §1 forbids a frontend agent learning a response
  shape from backend source — TASK-0054 would have had no sanctioned way to know the body is CSV.
  Cause: the non-generic `.Produces(200, contentType:)` overload supplies no response `Type`, and
  the generator drops `content` for an untyped response even when given a media type.
  `.Produces<string>(...)` fixed it in one line, no transformer, no shared file, now regression-
  guarded by a test asserting on the generated document.
  **Generalisable: `ci.ps1`'s contract-drift gate proves the document matches the BUILD, not that
  the document is complete.** A response the code produces but the metadata never declares is
  invisible to it. Reviewing the promoted document's own shape stays a human/orchestrator step.

  **Closure needed two gate runs; the first failure was infrastructure and was not treated as a
  defect.** One test (`RoleEndpointsTests`, unrelated) died with a transport drop inside
  `ApiTestFixture.ReseedClassLevelsAsync` during `InitializeAsync()` — before any assertion — after
  49 m 29 s, on code that had passed 902/902 an hour earlier. No VPN up, so not the port-filtering
  signature (which fails every integration test). Confirmed idle, re-ran, clean.

  **Two items raised out of this card, neither a defect in it:** TASK-0053 (CSV formula injection —
  RFC 4180 quoting does not stop formula interpretation, and `user_agent` is attacker-controlled
  with failed sign-ins audited, so the victim is the product's most privileged reader; found in
  review, not reported by the agent) and open question 14 (no `entityId` filter, so 6.1.12's
  own motivating scenario is not expressible — escalated, since filter dimensions are product
  behaviour). Accepted drift: the GET-that-writes CSRF surface and the raw-JSON-text
  `beforeJson`/`afterJson` typing, both in `## Known drift` with triggers.

- 2026-09-09 **TASK-0049 implemented by backend-dev — audit log read surface and CSV export
  (spec 6.1.12), both new endpoints built exactly to the card's contract delta. Status table and
  closure left to the orchestrator per this dispatch's instructions.**

  `GET /api/v1/audit-events` (`audit.view`, cursor-paginated per spec 9.5) and
  `GET /api/v1/audit-events/export` (`audit.export`, streamed `text/csv`) — route names follow the
  TASK-0049 card's own contract delta table verbatim (`/audit-events*`), which differs from spec
  6.1.14's shorthand listing (`GET /audit`, `GET /audit/export`); the card is the authority per this
  dispatch's own instructions, so no escalation was raised over the naming difference.

  **The tie-break (the card's named crux).** `occurred_at` is not unique, so the read surface sorts
  `occurred_at DESC, id DESC` — a NEW dedicated codec, `AuditEventListCursor` (composite
  `(DateTimeOffset, long)`, base64 of `ticks<0x1F>id`), not a reuse of `OpaqueCursor` (single key)
  or `AdminAccountListCursor` (string tie-break needing raw SQL). Since both fields are ordinary
  scalars, plain LINQ expresses the keyset predicate directly
  (`OccurredAt < cursor || (OccurredAt == cursor && Id < cursorId)`) — no raw SQL needed, unlike
  `AdminAccountRepository.ListAsync`'s row-value comparison for its string tie-break.
  `AuditEventListCursorTests.TwoRowsWithTheIdenticalOccurredAt_EncodeToDifferentCursors` plus
  `AuditEventEndpointsTests.List_RowsWithIdenticalOccurredAt_ArePagedInStableIdDescendingOrder`
  seed FIVE rows at one identical `occurred_at`, walk every page at `pageSize=2` against real
  Postgres, and assert the collected id sequence is the exact reverse-insertion-order set — no
  duplicate, no gap.

  **The export's self-log (the card's other named criterion).** `ExportAuditEventsCommand` is an
  `ICommand`, not a query — deliberately, because every call WRITES one `audit_event` row (action
  `audit.export`, via the existing `ISystemAuditSink.RecordAsync`, TASK-0048's mechanism) before
  returning the filtered stream. The handler builds the (unexecuted, lazy) `IAsyncEnumerable` from
  `IAuditEventQueryRepository.StreamAsync` and returns `Result.Success` alongside it;
  `UnitOfWorkBehavior` then commits the self-log INSERT before the endpoint ever starts writing CSV
  bytes to the response — "before returning" is true by construction, not by convention. The
  self-log's `after_json` records all six filters, present-but-null when unfiltered, so an
  unfiltered export and a narrowed one are distinguishable after the fact — proven by
  `Export_WritesItsOwnAuditEventRecordingTheFiltersUsed_DistinguishableFromAnUnfilteredExport`,
  which reads the self-log back through this card's OWN `GET /audit-events?action=audit.export`
  rather than a raw query.

  **CSV.** Hand-rolled RFC 4180 encoding (`AuditEventCsvWriter`, ~45 lines) — no new package
  dependency for thirteen well-known columns. `TypedResults.Stream(Func<Stream,Task>, "text/csv")`
  (`PushStreamHttpResult`) writes rows as they are pulled off the lazy `IAsyncEnumerable`
  (`AsNoTracking` all the way down), never materialising the filtered set as a list — the card's
  "stream, don't buffer" instruction. Columns are exactly spec 6.1.12's thirteen; `source_ip` is
  emitted as already truncated (stored truncated by TASK-0048, never re-widened here).

  **Two ports, deliberately kept separate.** `IAuditEventRepository` (TASK-0048) stays add-only,
  exactly as its own remarks reserve; this card's reads go through a NEW
  `IAuditEventQueryRepository` (`ListAsync` + `StreamAsync`), so the append-only guarantee cannot be
  quietly reopened by a read-surface change.

  **A schema-generation quirk changed one design choice.** `AuditEventDto.Outcome` is typed as the
  real `AuditOutcome` enum (matching `AdminAccountSummaryDto.Status`/`PupilDto.Status` precedent,
  not `string` as first drafted) because a NULLABLE enum used ONLY as a `[FromQuery]` parameter
  (never non-nullably anywhere else) produced a component schema with no XML-doc description or
  example — .NET's OpenAPI XML-comment enrichment appears not to reach that particular nullable-only
  shape. Giving `Outcome` one non-nullable DTO-property usage fixed it and is the better convention
  match anyway. Separately, `BeforeJson`/`AfterJson` are plain `string?` (raw JSON text), NOT
  `JsonElement?` like `ConfigVersionDetailDto.Snapshot` — introducing a SECOND and THIRD use of
  `JsonElement` made the generator hoist a shared, description-less `JsonElement` component (the
  existing single-use case stays inline and keeps its description attached at the property level,
  so this had never surfaced before). Both are recorded as deviations below rather than spending a
  dispatch inside `SchemaExampleTransformer.cs`, a shared cross-cutting Api file, to fix a generator
  internal this card does not otherwise need to touch.

  **Assumption, stated plainly (no entityId filter).** 6.1.14's prose example ("exporting one
  pupil's rows") reads as if a per-entity filter existed, but the card's contract delta table lists
  exactly five filters — `fromUtc`/`toUtc`, `actorAdminId`, `action`, `entityType`, `outcome` — with
  no `entityId`. Built exactly those five; `entityType=pupil` is the closest available narrowing.
  Not escalated because the card's own text ("The card's acceptance criteria are the spec") settles
  it; flagged here in case entityId filtering was actually intended and simply left off the table.

  **Assumption: no CSRF token or Idempotency-Key on `GET /audit-events/export`** despite it writing
  a row. Every existing GET route in this codebase (`ListAdminAccounts`, `ListPupils`, `GetPupil`,
  `FindPupilDuplicates`, …) omits both, and both mechanisms are otherwise reserved for
  POST/PATCH/DELETE routes; inventing a per-route exception for the one GET with a side effect would
  itself be the "second convention" root CLAUDE.md §13 warns against. Flagged rather than decided
  silently — a `SameSite=Lax` cookie still rides a top-level GET navigation, so this is a real CSRF
  surface if the frontend ever triggers the download via `window.location` rather than a
  fetch+blob pattern; worth a look when the frontend card for this screen is scoped.

  **Size**: ~780 hand-written production lines (Domain: none needed; Application 335, Infrastructure
  191, Api 214, excluding tests) — under the card's ~900-line stop-and-report threshold, no split
  needed. Test lines (not counted against that budget): 365 (`AuditEventEndpointsTests`) + 84
  (`AuditEventListCursorTests`) + 4 (a `PipelineTests` DI-stub addition, see below).

- 2026-09-09 **TASK-0050 implemented by backend-dev — pupil entity, the pending-exclusion invariant,
  and the register read surface. Status table and closure left to the orchestrator per this
  dispatch's instructions.**

  **The pending-exclusion invariant (the card's first named criterion), built as a MODEL-LEVEL EF
  Core query filter, not a per-query `.Where()`.** `PupilConfiguration.HasQueryFilter(pupil =>
  pupil.Status != PupilStatus.Pending)` — the identical, already-reviewed mechanism
  `ApplicationDbContext.ApplySoftDeleteQueryFilters` uses for `ISoftDeletable`, applied directly to
  `Pupil` rather than by reflection over an interface. Three deliberate `.IgnoreQueryFilters()` opt-outs,
  each with its own reason: direct-id lookups (finding a pending record to edit it IS this card's
  goal), `GET /pupils?status=Pending` (the one opt-out the contract itself names), and `GET
  /admissions`/`GET /pupils/duplicates` (the queue and duplicate detection both need pending rows by
  design). **Proven, not asserted**: `PupilEndpointsTests.List_WithoutStatus_ExcludesAPendingPupil_
  ButAdmissionsQueueShowsIt` seeds one pending pupil and shows the SAME record absent from the default
  list, present under `status=Pending`, and present in `GET /admissions` — three assertions against one
  seed, so the divergence is provably the filter, not different data.

  **Arm-scoped `pupil.view`/`pupil.update` (the card's second named criterion) — real tension the card
  did not fully anticipate, resolved and disclosed rather than guessed at.** Spec 6.5.3 calls the
  privilege "arm-scoped for a Class Teacher," but 6.5.4's own entity table carries NO arm reference —
  a pupil's arm comes only from its open enrolment (spec 07 line 9), which this card's own hard
  boundary forbids building. The existing route-declarative scope mechanism
  (`RequirePrivilege(..., ScopeParameterKind.Pupil, "id")`, resolving via `IPupilArmOfRecordLookup`)
  would therefore fail EVERY caller closed — including a school-wide holder — making
  `PATCH /pupils/{id}` unusable for its own stated purpose. Resolution: `GET /pupils`,
  `GET/PATCH /pupils/{id}` map with `.RequireAuthenticatedCaller()` and resolve privilege+scope in the
  HANDLER via a new `PupilAccessGuard`, the same "data-dependent privilege" pattern
  `UpdateAdminAccountCommandHandler` already established for spec 6.1.2's self-edit carve-out — using
  `IEffectivePrivilegeProvider` directly, never re-deriving arm resolution, per the card's own
  instruction. Since no pupil carries an arm today, an arm-scoped-only grant resolves to an honest
  empty page (list) or 403 (single-resource) — real, tested, and disclosed as a live drift trigger
  (this file's `## Known drift`) rather than presented as if it does something more today. **Proven
  against the REAL, DI-registered `RoleAssignmentEffectivePrivilegeProvider`, not a fake, both
  directions**: `List_ArmScopedCaller_SeesAnEmptyPage_SchoolWideCallerSeesTheRecord` and
  `Get_ArmScopedCaller_Returns403_SchoolWideCallerReturns200` seed a real `role_assignment` row
  directly through the DbContext (the same accepted technique `AssignmentEndpointsTests.
  SeedAssignmentAsync` uses) and sign in for real over HTTP — both callers query the SAME seeded
  record, so the divergent 200-vs-empty / 200-vs-403 outcome is caused by the scope check itself.
  `POST /pupils` and `GET /pupils/duplicates` need none of this — `pupil.create` is not scopable
  (spec 4.4.4) — and stay on the ordinary declarative `RequirePrivilege(...)` gate. `GET /admissions`
  is declared `RequirePrivilege(Pupil.View, ScopeParameterKind.None)` (school-wide only), an authored
  reading disclosed in `ASSUMPTIONS.md` §2.27, not a spec sentence.

  **`PrivilegeDecision.cs:21`'s live drift trigger fired and is RE-POINTED, not resolved** — see this
  file's `## Known drift` and `ASSUMPTIONS.md` §2.27 for the full reasoning:
  `IPupilArmOfRecordLookup` is still not implementable for real without an enrolment.
  `NotYetImplementedPupilArmOfRecordLookup` is untouched.

  **Entity, per 6.5.4, no more and no less**: `surname`/`first_name`/`middle_name` (letters/spaces/
  hyphens/apostrophes, `[GeneratedRegex]`-checked), `sex`, `date_of_birth` (age 2-20 inclusive,
  6.5.4's verbatim rejection message reformatted with real values —
  `PupilTests.Create_WithAnAgeBelowTheMinimum_RejectsWithTheVerbatimMessageShape` asserts the exact
  string), `nationality` (defaults `Nigerian`), `state_of_origin`/`lga` (closed-list, never free text —
  new `Domain/Pupils/NigerianGeography.cs`, 37 states + 774 LGAs, LGA data flagged unverified in
  `## Known drift`), `home_address`, `previous_school`/`previous_class`, `status` (defaults `Pending`,
  every other member exists for the column's shape only — no status-change endpoint), `other_information`.
  `registration_number` nullable + UNIQUELY indexed now (`ix_pupils_registration_number_unique`) so
  TASK-0051 alters nothing; no setter exists anywhere on the type outside the private constructor
  (`PupilTests`' reflective proof). No `blood_group`/`genotype`/`medical_note` (moved to
  `pupil_health`, next card), no `photograph` column (upload out of scope; a column nobody can
  populate is a stub the card's own guidance says to omit).

  **`PATCH /pupils/{id}` rejects a `registrationNumber` in the payload with 409**, tested
  (`Update_SettingRegistrationNumber_Returns409`) — the command declares the field only so its
  presence can be detected and refused, never written.

  **Search matches surname/first/middle name and the registration number** (full or "serial alone" —
  satisfied by ordinary substring matching, since `"41"` is literally a substring of `"...0041"`; no
  separate serial-extraction step was needed, reasoning and one accepted looseness in `ASSUMPTIONS.md`
  §2.27). `PupilDto.MatchedField` names which field matched. Contact/pickup-person search explicitly
  NOT covered — documented in the endpoint's own `WithDescription`, per the card's own instruction to
  "say so in the response shape."

  **Audit**: `POST /pupils` and `PATCH /pupils/{id}` call `ISystemAuditSink.RecordAsync`; `GET
  /pupils`, `GET /pupils/{id}` and `GET /admissions` do NOT — proven by
  `PupilEndpointsTests.Update_HappyPath_ChangesHomeAddressAndIsAudited` (one record, right action/
  entity id) and `List_And_Get_AreNotAudited` (zero `pupil`-typed records across three read calls,
  filtered past an unrelated background `system.idempotency_purge` event the same run legitimately
  produced).

  **Cursor pagination is real LINQ over `context.Pupils`, deliberately NOT
  `Database.SqlQuery<T>`** (`AdminAccountRepository.ListAsync`'s own pattern) — `SqlQuery<T>`
  materialises into an unmapped POCO with no entity-model context, so the model-level pending filter
  cannot compose onto it; using it would have silently defeated the invariant this card exists to
  build. The composite `(surname, id)` keyset comparison `AdminAccountRepository`'s own comment says
  C# cannot express with a relational operator on `string` is instead written as
  `string.Compare(...) > 0`/`Guid.CompareTo(...) > 0` inside the LINQ predicate — the Npgsql provider
  DOES translate this to SQL, verified empirically against real Postgres by the list/search tests
  actually executing it, not assumed from documentation. **Default sort is surname-then-id, not spec
  6.5.15's "class in progression order then surname ascending"** — no class/arm reference exists on
  `Pupil` yet; disclosed as a live drift trigger rather than silently substituted.

  **`GET /pupils/duplicates`** matches surname AND first name AND date of birth, INCLUDING pending
  records (catching a second in-progress admission is the point), gated `pupil.create`. Contact-phone
  matching (spec 6.5.11's other half) is the next card's, once `pupil_contact` exists.

- 2026-09-09 **Rejected audit events are written on their own connection, NOT the ambient
  transaction. HUMAN SIGN-OFF 2026-09-09.** Spec 14 §9.3 ("the audit write shares the transaction
  with the change it records") and spec 03 §6.1.12 ("Rejected entries are written for privilege
  failures and for escalation attempts") are in direct conflict here, because
  `UnitOfWork.ExecuteAtomicallyAsync` rolls back on a failure `Result` — so a rejection event added
  to the DbContext is rolled back and lost. Ruling: a rejection records NO change, so §9.3's clause
  does not bind it; `outcome = success` events join the ambient transaction, `outcome = rejected`
  events are written synchronously on a separate short-lived connection that commits immediately.
  Still no queue, no fire-and-forget, no separate service. **Why this mattered enough to escalate:
  TASK-0030's rejection tests assert against a `RecordingSystemAuditSink` fake that has no
  transaction, so the literal §9.3 implementation would have left every one of those tests GREEN
  while production silently lost every rejection record** — the same "a green result that means
  nothing" class as all of Phase 0b. Owner TASK-0048.
- 2026-09-09 **`audit_event` append-only is enforced at BOTH layers, and the two-role provisioning
  is deliberate drift. HUMAN SIGN-OFF 2026-09-09.** The migration emits `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE ON
  audit_event` against the application role so a correctly-provisioned deployment gets the real
  §9.3 database guarantee; the application layer additionally exposes no update or delete path on
  the audit repository, held by an architecture test so it cannot be added back quietly. The
  separate administrative role for retention pruning is NOT provisioned on the hosted Neon test
  database or in CI — both connect as an owner role that a `REVOKE` cannot bind, so locally and in
  CI the code-level guarantee is the operative one. Recorded in `## Known drift`, owner TASK-0048.

- 2026-09-09 **TASK-0048 implemented by backend-dev — `audit_event` persistence and the append-only
  guarantee, both human-signed rulings above carried out. Status table and closure left to the
  orchestrator per this dispatch's instructions.**

  Both `LoggingSystemAuditSink` and `LoggingAuthorizationAuditSink` **DELETED** (git `D`), both
  `TODO(TASK-0002)` markers gone with them. Still exactly two Application-facing audit abstractions:
  `ISystemAuditSink` gained a second method, `RecordRejectionAsync` (identical parameter shape to
  `RecordAsync`), rather than an `outcome` flag on one method — the two outcomes persist through
  entirely different mechanisms, so two methods made that visible at the call site rather than
  buried in a branch. `IAuthorizationAuditSink`'s single method is unchanged in shape.

  **Ruling 1, proven against Postgres, not `RecordingSystemAuditSink`** — the criterion the card
  exists for. `RecordAsync` (success) adds to the ambient `DbContext`'s change tracker with no
  `SaveChangesAsync`, committed by `UnitOfWorkBehavior` alongside the change. `RecordRejectionAsync`
  builds a FRESH `ApplicationDbContext` (new `DbContextOptionsBuilder`, same `DatabaseOptions`) via a
  new `RejectedAuditEventWriter` and calls a bare `SaveChangesAsync` — no explicit transaction, so
  EF Core's own `EnableRetryOnFailure` execution strategy applies automatically without hand-rolling a
  raw `NpgsqlConnection`, per the card's own instruction. `AssignmentEndpointsTests` gained two new
  facts using the REAL DI-registered sink (no `RemoveAll<ISystemAuditSink>()` swap):
  `Create_RuleOne_SelfAssignment_PersistsADurableRejectedAuditEventRow` and
  `Create_RuleThree_ActorArmScopedNarrowerThanRequested_PersistsADurableRejectedAuditEventRow` — each
  drives the real HTTP endpoint to a 403, then opens a fresh scope and queries `context.AuditEvents`
  directly, asserting exactly one row with `Outcome == Rejected`. A new
  `AuditEventPersistenceTests.PrivilegeMiddlewareRejection_PersistsADurableRejectedAuditEventRow` does
  the same for `PrivilegeAuthorizationHandler` (outside any MediatR pipeline, no ambient transaction
  at all), leaving `IAuthorizationAuditSink` un-swapped for the first time in that test family. Two
  more facts in the same file prove the success-path half directly against `IUnitOfWork`: a
  successful atomic operation commits both an entity and its audit row together; one that THROWS
  mid-way (not a failure `Result` — a genuine exception) leaves neither behind.

  **~7 of the ~36 existing call sites renamed `RecordAsync` → `RecordRejectionAsync`** — method name
  only, no parameter list touched at any of them: `CreateRoleCommandHandler`/
  `UpdateRoleCommandHandler` (rule 2), `CreateRoleAssignmentCommandHandler` (rule 1, rule 3),
  `RevokeRoleAssignmentCommandHandler` (rule 1 on revoke), `UpdateAdminAccountCommandHandler`
  (`admin.super_admin_grant_denied`), and — beyond the card's two NAMED criteria, flagged rather than
  silently folded in — `UpdateSchoolIdentityCommandHandler`
  (`settings.identity.save_rejected_stale_version`), which also precedes a `Result.Failure` and would
  otherwise have silently regressed from the log-only seam's own non-transactional behaviour. The
  remaining ~29 call sites are untouched; no 36-site mechanical edit was needed or done.

  **Ruling 2**: migration emits `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE ON TABLE audit_event FROM CURRENT_USER;` (and
  the `Down()` GRANT back) — targets `CURRENT_USER`, not a named role, since no separate application
  role is provisioned anywhere this migration runs; a harmless no-op against the table owner (Neon,
  CI), a real guarantee the moment a deployment provisions a genuinely separate role.
  `IAuditEventRepository` exposes only `AddAsync`; a new architecture test
  (`AuditAppendOnlyTests.IAuditEventRepository_HasNoUpdateOrDeleteMethod`) reflects over the interface
  and fails if any other method name ever appears on it.

  **Thirteen fields, all present.** `id` BIGSERIAL identity (`long`, EF Core's ordinary
  `ValueGeneratedOnAdd`, stated explicitly — the first non-Guid-v7 key in the schema). `actor_label`
  resolved at write time via `IAdminAccountRepository.FindReadOnlyByIdAsync`, never joined on read —
  proven by `ActorLabel_IsCapturedAtWriteTime_NotJoinedOnRead` (audit, rename the account, re-read:
  label still shows the ORIGINAL name/email). `source_ip`/`user_agent` truncated by a new pure
  `AuditFieldTruncation` static class (IPv4 → /24, IPv6 → /48, IPv4-mapped IPv6 unwrapped first,
  `user_agent` cut at 300, never throws) — 12 unit tests. `ICurrentUser` gained
  `RemoteIpAddress`/`UserAgent` (raw, untruncated) rather than a new Infrastructure package
  reference for `IHttpContextAccessor` — `HttpCurrentUser` (Api) implements both over its existing
  accessor. `reason` is a new trailing OPTIONAL parameter on both `ISystemAuditSink` methods, placed
  AFTER `CancellationToken` (every existing call site passes it as the last positional argument;
  inserting an optional parameter before it would have silently rebound that value) — threaded for

  `after_json` columns added, unpopulated (out of scope, forward obligation recorded below and in
  `docs/ASSUMPTIONS.md` §2.25); `after_json` reuses the pre-existing `metadata` argument for the

  **Temporary password handling (the card's own emphasis)**: `useCreateAdmin`/`useResetAdminPassword`
  both set `gcTime: 0` — discovered necessary because `useMutation`'s `reset()` only detaches the
  observer, it does NOT evict the underlying `Mutation` from `queryClient`'s mutation cache (that
  needs zero observers AND its `gcTime` to elapse); without `gcTime: 0` the plaintext value would
  have lingered in the mutation cache for the default 5 minutes after the dialog closed. Both
  dialogs (`CreateAdminDialog`, `ResetPasswordDialog`) copy `temporaryPassword` into local
  component state in the SAME tick as calling `.reset()`, never read it back from the mutation's
  own `data` again, and the query cache never receives it at all — `AdminAccountDetailDto`/
  `AdminAccountSummaryDto` (every subsequent read's shape) structurally has no such field.
  `admins-list-screen.test.tsx` and `admin-detail-screen.test.tsx` both assert the value is absent
  from the DOM and from `queryClient.getQueryCache()`/`getMutationCache()` after the dialog closes.
