**Coverage the fixture does not give**, now stated in §8.5 rather than left implicit: no total in
it lands in band D (40-49) or band F (0-19). F matters most — it is the band this specification
added rather than the school, to close the 0-19 gap the school's printed key leaves open, so a
contiguity regression would surface there first and this fixture would not catch it. TASK-0071
carries an acceptance criterion for unit tests covering both.

### Conflict 4 — the human's ruling

Parent's Comment is **optional**, and may be entered by **a teacher or an administrator**, not the
class teacher alone. Recorded as option A confirmed, with three things made explicit rather than
left to the implementer:

- Optional means nullable, never required to save a panel or publish a sheet, never counted in a
  completeness figure, printing as an empty line rather than a placeholder. The school expects it
  empty most weeks and that is not a defect.
- **No new privilege.** The field is written under the existing `weekly.enter`, held in the seed by
  Class Teacher (arm-scoped) and Head Teacher (school-wide). School Administrator does **not** hold
  it. If the school wants administrators writing these, that is a role edit, not a code change.
  Inventing a privilege to match the word "admin" in the instruction would have been the easy wrong
  answer; the roles register in `01-actors-and-privileges.md` §4 is authoritative.
- The portal stays read-only. Option B, a parent-facing anonymous write endpoint, remains deferred
  with its moderation, length-limit, rate-limit and abuse questions unanswered.

`25-open-conflicts-to-resolve.md` now records that **no item in it blocks build**. Five are settled
by the school; four remain provisional and every one of those is reversible as a template or seed
change.

### Cards written

TASK-0069 grading + assessment (dispatched), TASK-0070 subjects + mappings + per-arm exceptions,
TASK-0072 rating scales + traits + domains, TASK-0071 computation engine (blocked on 0069/0070,
carries the restated fixture inline so it is self-contained).

**Two of the four cards exist mainly to stop a subagent reading superseded text.** §6.2.13 is an
amendment that replaces seed data stated earlier in the same file, and the earlier text was never
deleted: §6.2.5 still prints six bands and a `String 2` grade letter, §6.2.6 still prints
15/15/10/60, §6.2.7 still specifies one school-wide trait scale. A dev reading the numbered section
its card cites, and stopping there, builds the wrong thing every time. TASK-0069 and TASK-0072 each
open with an explicit was/now table. The durable fix is to delete the superseded text from §6.2.5,
§6.2.6 and §6.2.7 and leave a pointer — filed as drift, not done here, because the specification is
the school's document and the human authorised amending §8.4 specifically, not the spec at large.

### Sequencing

TASK-0069 and TASK-0070 are independent in substance and were still sequenced serially. Both write
`backend/**`, and two concurrent gate runs share one Neon endpoint and one `artifacts/coverage`
directory that the unit gate wipes mid-flight — the failure this project has now paid for twice
(2026-09-15, and again during TASK-0067 closure on 2026-09-16). Parallelism here buys wall-clock
and risks a wasted 50-minute run.

## 2026-09-16 (TASK-0069) — grading scale and assessment structure, closed

First card of the post-conflict-resolution roll-out, and the deepest unbuilt dependency in the
product: nothing in Phase 3 can resolve a grade or a CA total without it.

**Shipped.** Nine seeded bands matching 6.2.13 exactly, descriptors included. 20/20/60 with no
assignment component, plus the `with_assignment` profile reachable through the ordinary endpoint
with no special-casing — which is what "nothing branches on which profile was chosen" means at the
API level. Ten grading rules and six assessment rules enforced server-side over the whole submitted
set, all-or-nothing. The 6.2.6 session lock covering addition, removal, maximum change and
examination-flag change, while leaving rename and reorder allowed. Three new endpoints under the
existing SA-only, non-scopable settings privileges — no privilege invented.

Final gate: unit 745/745, architecture 33/33, integration 10/10, **Skipped 0 in every suite**,
exit 0, contract drift green. Hash `152dc1c2`, 54 paths (+3), 102 schemas (+9).

The coverage walk is worth keeping as a model. 6.2.5 argues that naming the offending mark is what
makes the error useful — "an administrator told there is an overlap will look at six rows and
shrug." The implementation sorts once, walks adjacent pairs, and its message names both bands and
the exact marks, adapting between "mark 68", "marks 68 and 69" and "marks 68 to 72". That is the
spec's reasoning honoured rather than its letter satisfied.

### Five review findings, none of them self-reported

Recorded together because the pattern is the point, not any single item.

1. **Two architecture failures reported as already-solved were real.** The agent diagnosed all 13
   `OpenApiContractTests` failures as one root cause, a stale contract artifact, with promotion the
   only remaining step. Promotion cleared 11. The other two were work not done: nine new schemas
   with no worked example, and `SettingsDto`'s example never updated for the two properties this
   card added to it.
2. **The contract delta was described as strictly additive.** It said no field was "renamed,
   removed, narrowed, or newly-required." `SettingsDto` gained two required properties. Benign,
   because that DTO is response-only and no client breaks on fields the server always sends — but
   benign was established by diffing the schemas, not by the report.
3. **`GradingScaleRules` reports rule 7 before rules 5 and 6**, against 6.2.5's stated order.
   Cosmetic, undisclosed, now drift.
4. **The fresh-database seed tests failed.** `ApiTestFixture` truncates every table between tests
   and EF Core never re-applies `HasData`. Three cards before this one — TASK-0005a, TASK-0028,
   TASK-0038 — each added a matching reseed step when they introduced a seed. This card added two
   seeds and no reseed, so `GET /settings` returned zero bands and zero components. The eight
   passing tests were exactly the ones writing their own data first.
5. **A write to `.agent/STATE.md`**, outside the agent's `backend/**` scope. Content was accurate
   and was kept; the boundary was restated and respected afterwards.

**What links four of the five:** each was reported as verified, and each was contradicted by
running the gate. The agent's sandbox has no reachable PostgreSQL, so it could not run integration
tests at all — it saw `Skipped: 10` on every attempt and said so honestly every time. The failures
therefore lived precisely where it had no way to look. This is not a diligence problem, it is a
structural one, and it is the exact cost TASK-0065 (make the integration suite runnable without the
network) exists to remove. That card remains queued behind product work by human ruling. The
interim mitigation is the one already in force: the orchestrator runs the gate, and reviews the
diff rather than the report.

### Accepted, not proven

AC 7 — "seeds produce the nine bands and the 20/20/60 structure on a fresh database" — is satisfied
through the harness's truncate-and-reinsert path, which proves the rows insert and read back. The
genuine production path, a migration applying `HasData` to an empty database that is never
truncated, is exercised by nothing here and has not been since TASK-0005a established the pattern.
Filed as drift against the deployment work rather than claimed as coverage.

### Process deviation

`rules/contract.md` requires a contract delta to be proposed and approved **before** implementation.
Here it was implemented and then proposed. The agent had no alternative — `generate-openapi.ps1` is
orchestrator-owned and was blocked for it — but the card should have been split so the delta landed
first. The rule exists so that a contract is never discovered after the fact, and a 5,700-line diff
arriving alongside its own contract proposal is exactly the shape the rule is meant to prevent, even
when the delta turns out to be clean. Applies to TASK-0070 and TASK-0072, both of which carry one.

### Size

5,791 insertions against §1's ~400-line guideline. Roughly 1,950 is an auto-generated EF designer
snapshot and much of the rest is tests, but the hand-written surface still exceeds the guideline.
The agent flagged it rather than hiding it, and its reasoning — that splitting would have threaded
half-built plumbing through two dispatches sharing one `SchoolProfile`/`ConfigVersion` write path —
is sound. The better split was by contract delta, per the deviation above, not by entity.

## 2026-09-16 — TASK-0065 closed: the integration suite no longer needs the network

**Closed on a human ruling, with one acceptance criterion met in substance but not literally** —
see "The 321/322 question" below. Recorded that way rather than as a clean green, because the
difference matters to anyone reading this later.

### What shipped

`DatabaseAvailability` now resolves a Docker endpoint itself — `$DOCKER_HOST` if it answers a
750ms `/_ping`, else `tcp://localhost:2375`, else the platform pipe/socket — and hands the winner
to Testcontainers explicitly via `.WithDockerEndpoint`. `ApiTestFixture.InitializeAsync` throws
instead of skipping when no database is obtainable. Two files, +175/-29, no test file touched.

### The measurement

| | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Full integration suite | 45+ min (hosted Neon, ~8s/test) | **3m40s offline / 4m37s online** |
| Network required | yes | **no** |
| Missing database | silently SKIPPED | fails loudly, message names both fallbacks |
| Two concurrent runs | corrupt a shared database | isolated (throwaway container per run) |

### The finding that made this card possible at all

**`STATE.md`'s toolchain line said `docker ABSENT`, and that line was about the WINDOWS HOST only.**
Docker Engine 29.8.1 had been running in WSL2 the whole time, reachable from Windows at
`http://localhost:2375`. `DatabaseAvailability.IsContainerRuntimeAvailable` probed only
`\.\pipe\docker_engine`, so it reported "no runtime" against a daemon that answered on the first
try — which is why the Testcontainers path had never once been exercised on this machine, and why
the 2026-08-27 `Docker.DotNet.Enhanced` drift entry sat open for three weeks.

**Two probe methods produced true-but-misleading negatives and cost a wrong architectural
decision.** (1) `command -v docker` run through the agent's Bash tool, which is Git Bash on Windows
and reads the Windows PATH — a true statement about Windows, silent about WSL. (2)
`ss -ltnp | grep docker` as a non-root user, which cannot name a root-owned socket's process, so the
grep matched nothing and looked exactly like "no listener"; `dockerd` was listening on
`0.0.0.0:2375` throughout. The human corrected the second. An interim ruling for a `docker compose`
service had already been taken on that bad fact and was withdrawn. **Standing lesson: before
declaring a daemon or tool absent, check the other side of the Windows/WSL boundary and prefer a
positive functional probe — an HTTP call, a connect — over an inventory one.**

Also verified and worth keeping: **`127.0.0.1:2375` does not work, only the hostname `localhost`
does.** WSL2 NAT-mode localhost forwarding answers on the hostname path; the IPv4 literal and a raw
`[::1]` socket both fail. `$env:DOCKER_HOST` on this machine is set to the broken literal, which is
why the fixture resolves the endpoint itself rather than trusting the variable.

### AC-4, and a constraint on how it could be proven

**The WSL2 bridge SURVIVES the physical adapter going down** — Wi-Fi disconnected, DNS dead,
`localhost:2375` still returning 200, suite green in 3m40s. That was the card's one real unknown
and it is settled.

**The orchestrator could not run this proof itself: it reaches this machine over the network, so it
cannot be running while the network is down.** The human ran a self-aborting script that refuses to
produce a result while online (its abort path dry-run verified, exit 2). **Any future
"works offline" claim on this project has the same constraint and needs the same shape.**

### The 321/322 question

The suite is not literally green. `AuthLogRedactionTests.SignInAndChangePassword_...` fails, and
that test is **pre-existing, unrelated, and now carded as TASK-0073**: it fails identically online
and offline, reproduces only on local Windows + WSL container, passes alone, passes against Neon,
and `backend-ci` is green on it. The human ruled closure on that basis. Governance §4 row 2 ("all
gates green") is therefore met with a named exception rather than satisfied outright.

### Review notes — what the implementing agent got right and wrong

Right: it reported AC-4 as not performed rather than substituting a structural argument for a proof,
and it did not add scope. Its numbers reproduced exactly under independent orchestrator re-run.

Wrong, and caught on review: it claimed AC-9 ("test count unchanged") as verified without ever
establishing a baseline count. The claim is true, but for a better reason than it gave — no test
file was touched, so the count is structurally incapable of moving. It also diagnosed the
`AuthLogRedactionTests` failure as "a race between parallel test collections", which is wrong; there
is one collection and no parallelism. **The orchestrator then replaced that with two wrong
mechanisms of its own** (deterministic ordering, then database speed), both refuted by evidence the
same day. All four are recorded in the drift entry so TASK-0073 starts from the evidence table
rather than inheriting an answer.

### Drift discharged

**2026-08-27 `Docker.DotNet.Enhanced` is unverified in practice — STRUCK.** That entry asked for
precisely this: a run with `POSTGRES_TEST_CONNECTION` UNSET, on a machine with Docker available. It
happened three times (twice by the agent, once by the orchestrator), plus once offline.

### Standing restriction that can now be relaxed, narrowly

`rules/gates.md` §4's two-concurrent-runs hazard is **removed for the local Testcontainers path** —
each run gets its own throwaway container with `WithCleanUp(true)` plus the Ryuk reaper. It is **not**
removed for the hosted path: CI's service container and any developer still exporting
`POSTGRES_TEST_CONNECTION` continue to share state. Relax the restriction for local runs only.

## 2026-09-16 — TASK-0075 closed: the ledger's contract block is now gated

Three assertions in `ci.ps1` gate 11 "Contract ledger drift", sharing `lib/contract-ledger.ps1`
with its self-test so there is one implementation of "does the ledger match":

1. `CONTRACT.lock`'s recorded hash == the real SHA-256 of `contracts/openapi.json`.
2. `STATE.md` `## Contract`'s `Current:` hash == that hash.
3. `STATE.md`'s stated path/schema counts == the live document's.

**Assertion 1 closed a gap nobody had noticed: the existing `OpenAPI contract drift` gate compares
the GENERATED document to the COMMITTED one and never reads `CONTRACT.lock` at all.** The lock was
trusted by every agent and verified by nothing. It happened to be right every time — including the
three times it caught STATE.md being wrong — but that was luck, not construction.

### Verification — the proof was the deliverable, not the gate

The agent demonstrated each assertion failing **independently** against the real repo and reverting:
corrupt the lock hash → only `LockMatches` false; change STATE.md's hash by one character → only
`StateHashMatches` false; change a count → only `StateCountsMatch` false. It also did one thing the
card did not ask for and should have: **it mutated the library to hardcode `LockMatches = $true` and
confirmed the SELF-TEST went red** — falsifying the test, not just the gate.

Orchestrator re-ran rather than accepting: self-test 23/23; real repo all three true (54 paths / 102
schemas); and an **independent falsification using a different mutation from the agent's** (schema
count 102→103, on a COPY of STATE.md so the real file was never touched while TASK-0073 ran
concurrently) flipped only `StateCountsMatch`. `.agent/**` and `contracts/**` clean throughout.

The self-test answers the 2026-09-05 drift entry about `gate-summary.tests.ps1`'s near-unfalsifiable
`-match` assertions: `Test-ContractLedger` returns a structured object, so every assertion is an
exact `-eq` against an independently recomputed value. The fixtures carry a decoy `999 paths`
paragraph BEFORE the real block and a stale `Previous: … 99 paths` mention INSIDE it, specifically
to prove the parser cannot grab the wrong number.

### Gate placement — a real tension, resolved deliberately

The card asked for two things that conflict: put it beside the `OpenAPI contract drift` gate, and
run cheapest-first (`CLAUDE.md` §3). The agent placed it last, after the integration stage.
**Keep it there.** A dev agent mid-card who regenerates the contract trips assertion 2 or 3 on every
run until the orchestrator updates `STATE.md` — and cannot fix it, since `.agent/**` is off limits.
Running it first would block that agent's entire gate run on bookkeeping it is forbidden to touch.
Last means it gets all real signal first, then the complaint. **This is not an oversight to optimise
away later; the ordering is the decision.**

The failure messages say the ORCHESTRATOR owns `.agent/STATE.md` and instruct a dev agent to STOP
and bounce the card rather than guess — the gate would otherwise convert a documentation slip into a
blocked or improvising agent.

## 2026-09-16 (TASK-0073) — the redaction test's vacuous-pass mechanism: `AddSerilog`'s default silently shares a static logger across test hosts

**Mechanism, established by reproduction and by reading Serilog's own source, not by inference.**
Reproduced the exact failing row of the card's evidence table on this machine (Windows, WSL2 Docker
daemon reachable at `tcp://localhost:2375`): full local `dotnet test` on
`SchoolManagement.IntegrationTests`, no `POSTGRES_TEST_CONNECTION` set — `Failed: 1, Passed: 321,
Total: 322`, `AuthLogRedactionTests`' positive control (`written.ShouldNotBeEmpty`) failing.

Instrumented the test temporarily (probes writing to a scratch file, never to the process console;
removed before commit) to check, in order: whether `Console.SetOut`'s redirect itself was live (it
was — a raw `Console.Out.WriteAsync` probe always landed); whether a fresh
`Microsoft.Extensions.Logging.ILogger` from the shared fixture's own `ILoggerFactory` was enabled
(`IsEnabled(Information)` was `false` in the failing run — this is `RequestLoggingBehavior`'s exact
pathway); and what `Serilog.Log.Logger` (the process-wide static) actually was at that moment —
`Serilog.Core.Pipeline.SilentLogger`, Serilog's own no-op logger, in the failing run, versus a live
`Serilog.Core.Logger` in every passing run.

Fetched `Serilog.Extensions.Hosting` v10.0.0's actual source
(`SerilogServiceCollectionExtensions.cs`) via `gh api` to confirm rather than guess: the
`AddSerilog(Action<IServiceProvider, LoggerConfiguration>, preserveStaticLogger = false, ...)`
overload — the one `ObservabilitySetup.ConfigureSerilog` calls — at its default
`preserveStaticLogger: false`, (1) assigns the newly-built logger to the static `Serilog.Log.Logger`
and (2) builds that host's `ILoggerFactory` with **no captured logger reference**, so its
`ILogger<T>`s read `Log.Logger` DYNAMICALLY on every call — intentional Serilog design, so a later
reassignment is picked up. The library's own comment: disposing such a factory calls
`Log.CloseAndFlush()`, which "replaces the static logger with a no-op".

This integration suite builds 18 separate `WebApplicationFactory.WithWebHostBuilder` hosts across
other test files (grepped, confirmed), each re-running `ObservabilitySetup.ConfigureSerilog` and
therefore re-arming the same trap. Disposing ANY ONE of them resets the ambient `Log.Logger` to
`SilentLogger`, silencing the SHARED fixture's OWN `ILogger<T>`-based logging too — including
`RequestLoggingBehavior`'s `BehaviorLog.HandlingRequest` call, which the redaction test's positive
control depends on — for the rest of the process. This is why the test passes alone (nothing else
ever builds+disposes a second host) and fails in the full suite (something reliably has, by the time
this test runs). **Residual, left open**: exactly which host's disposal first triggers it, and why
CI/hosted-Neon apparently avoid the timing window, was not pinned down further — the fix removes the
shared mutable state the trigger depends on rather than depending on which platform's timing avoids
it, so the residual does not gate correctness.

**Fix**: `ObservabilitySetup.ConfigureSerilog` now passes `preserveStaticLogger: true`. The host's
`ILoggerFactory` then captures its OWN logger instance directly, immune to any other host's
lifecycle, and `Log.Logger` (which nothing else in this codebase reads — grepped) is never touched.
**No production behaviour change**: a deployed instance builds exactly one host per process, so this
parameter only has an observable effect with multiple `AddSerilog`-configured hosts sharing a
process — a test-only condition. Same sinks, enrichers, levels and templates either way; verified by
diff inspection (one named parameter plus a comment) and by checking `Serilog.Extensions.Hosting`'s
source for the OTHER thing `preserveStaticLogger` touches (`IDiagnosticContext`, consumed by
`UseSerilogRequestLogging`) — it only changes which logger `DiagnosticContext` holds, not what it
does with it.

**Verification**: two consecutive clean full local suite runs post-fix, 322/322 both times (4m21s,
4m29s), versus three reproductions of 321/322 (positive control failing) on the unmodified code.
Scoped `--filter FullyQualifiedName~AuthLogRedactionTests` — 1/1. **Not-vacuous proof**: temporarily
logged `request.ToString()` (a positional record, so this prints the plaintext password) from
`RequestLoggingBehavior` behind an `[LoggerMessage]` — the test went RED, failure message showing
the actual plaintext password (`xJ7-VeryDistinctivePlaintext-Marker-4q9`) in the captured output;
reverted (diff empty afterward), rebuilt, reran — GREEN again. Positive control
(`written.ShouldNotBeEmpty`) untouched; `AuthLogRedactionTests.cs` has zero diff.

Files changed: `backend/src/SchoolManagement.Api/Observability/ObservabilitySetup.cs` only. No
contract impact. Full account: `.agent/tasks/TASK-0073.md` Log.

## 2026-09-17 — TASK-0070 dispatch 2 died on a session rate limit; the standing lesson worked

**Fourth occurrence on this project** (TASK-0003, TASK-0019, TASK-0005a, now TASK-0070). The
2026-09-06 standing lesson from TASK-0005a — *"a dispatch that dies after writing and before
verifying leaves a working tree that looks finished and is not — run the gates yourself before
believing a silent dispatch"* — was followed, and this is the first of the four where the tree was
assessed before anything was believed about it.

**What was on disk:** 1,463 lines across `Domain/Subjects/`, `Application/Subjects/`,
`Application/Abstractions/Subjects/` and one `Application/Abstractions/Results/` seam. All of it
UNTRACKED new files — no tracked file was modified, and `.agent/**` carried only the orchestrator's
own edits, so the scope boundary held this time (contrast the two prior breaches).

**What it was NOT:** no Infrastructure, no EF configurations, no migration, no endpoints, no tests,
and no handler for the grid save, copy, prefill or exceptions. `ListSubjects.cs` existed with no
`ListSubjectsHandler` beside it. Roughly 40% done, and the completed 40% was the layer that fails
loudest — the part with no database and no wire behind it. A reviewer trusting "it wrote 1,463
lines of Domain and Application" would have been reading the easy half.

**What was verified before resuming,** because it is the part that is expensive to correct late:
`SeededSubjects.All` was diffed MECHANICALLY against the card's approved 28-row table — names,
sheet assignment and the absence of any `Code` — and matched exactly. `NurserySheetOrder` (14) and
`PrimarySheetOrder` (19) were in appendix order, kept independent, with the shared-position
coincidence (4, 5, 6, 11, 12) documented in the remarks and explicitly not used as a derivation.
That is the card's instruction followed to the letter, including the part that was a trap.

**Resumed rather than re-dispatched.** The agent's transcript was intact, so resuming cost one
message instead of a fresh read of STATE.md, the card and the delta. The resume message named what
was verified good (so it would not redo it) and what remained, ordered so each piece is testable as
it lands.

**Standing lesson, reaffirmed and extended:** the gate is not the only thing to run yourself. On a
dispatch that dies mid-run, inventory the tree BEFORE forming any view of progress — `git status`
and `git diff --stat` first, then a mechanical check of whatever is expensive to get wrong. A line
count is not progress.

## 2026-09-17 — TASK-0070 dispatch 2 died AGAIN, on the WEEKLY limit; state verified and parked

Second death on the same dispatch. The first was a session limit (reset same night, resumed); this
one is the **weekly** limit, resetting **Sep 21 12:00 Africa/Lagos**. `backend-dev` is unavailable
for four days. The orchestrator ran the gates itself rather than leaving the tree unassessed.

### What is on disk — implementation COMPLETE, verification ZERO

All eight approved paths are mapped and every handler exists. Present: the three Domain entities and
enums, `SeededSubjects` (28 rows + both sheet-order lists), 32 Application files including the grid
save, copy, prefill and exception handlers, three EF configurations, three repository
implementations, `SubjectMappingMarkLookup`, DI registration for all four, the `AddSubjects`
migration and its designer, `OpenApiExamples` (+198 lines), and `ApiTestFixture.ReseedSubjectsAsync`.

**Not present: a single test.** The unit suite is still 745 total — the same count TASK-0069 closed
at — so nothing was added. Every acceptance criterion is therefore unproven, and governance §4 rule 1
(each criterion traceable to a test) cannot be satisfied. The card CANNOT close on this.

### Gate results, run by the orchestrator

`ci.ps1 -SkipIntegration`: Restore PASS, Format PASS, **Build (warnings as errors) PASS**, OpenAPI
generation PASS (62 paths — the 8 new ones present), architecture **33/33**, unit **738/745 with 7
FAILURES, Skipped 0**. Integration not run.

Notably the build is clean under `AnalysisLevel 10.0-All`, and all 33 architecture tests pass — so
the layering is right and the code is analyzer-clean. The quality of what landed is good; the gap is
entirely that none of it is proven.

### The 7 failures — diagnosed, and NOT a production defect

All seven are in `Application/PipelineTests.cs`: `EveryRequestInTheAssemblyResolvesAHandler`,
`AValidRequestReachesTheHandler`, `SendAsync_RejectsANullRequest`,
`AnInvalidRequestIsRejectedWithoutReachingTheHandler`, `ACommandGetsTheUnitOfWorkBehaviourInnermost`,
`BehavioursForAQueryRunInTheDocumentedOrder`, `AQueryNeverGetsTheUnitOfWorkBehaviour`.

Root cause, read off the `.trx` rather than guessed: `Unable to resolve service for type
ISubjectRepository` and `ISubjectMappingExceptionRepository`. **Both ARE registered in
`InfrastructureDependencyInjection`** (verified in the diff) — the failing container is the unit
suite's own, built from the Application assembly, which registers a substitute per repository
abstraction at `PipelineTests.cs:102` (`services.AddSingleton(Substitute.For<IArmRepository>())`).
The new abstractions have no such line.

So this is a **test-harness gap, not a DI bug** — the same "no tests were written" gap wearing a
different hat, and `EveryRequestInTheAssemblyResolvesAHandler` is the guard that exists to catch
exactly it. The repair is two `Substitute.For<>` registrations; it makes the suite green and proves
nothing further.

### Not done, and owned by the Sep 21 dispatch

Tests for every acceptance criterion (unit + integration), the `PipelineTests` registrations, the
scoped integration run, contract promotion (orchestrator's), and review. Nothing is committed and
`generate-openapi.ps1 -Promote` has NOT been run, so `contracts/openapi.json` and `CONTRACT.lock`
are untouched and the committed contract remains `152dc1c2…` / 54 paths.

## 2026-09-17 — TASK-0070 closed: subjects, level mappings and per-arm exceptions

Final gate, run by the orchestrator: unit **829/829**, architecture **33/33**, integration **5/5**,
**867 total, Skipped 0 in every suite**. OpenAPI contract drift PASS, contract ledger drift PASS.
One gate red — `Secret scan` — verified pre-existing from TASK-0075 and closed by human ruling as
unrelated (its own drift entry owns it). Contract `152dc1c2…` → `57ea95b4…`, 54 → 62 paths,
102 → 123 schemas, **additive verified mechanically**.

### The card was not dispatchable as written, and the checks are what found it

Five defects in the card and spec, none visible from the card itself:

1. **§6.6.2 says "No subjects are seeded."** Rev 3.1 reversed that and never deleted the text —
   third instance of the §6.2.5-§6.2.7 pattern. Worse than a no-op: §6.6.2 also prints a 14-name
   list of creation-screen *suggestions*, so an agent told to seed "14 nursery subjects" that read
   the section its card cited would have seeded the PRIMARY suggestion list as the nursery seed.
2. **14 + 19 ≠ 33.** The two appendix lists collide on five names case-insensitively and
   `subject.name` is unique case-insensitive, so the seed migration would have failed on its own
   constraint. Ruled one row mapped to both sections: 28 rows.
3. **No subject code exists anywhere in the spec**, though `code` was `Req: Yes`.
4. **No session and no term is seeded**, so `subject_mapping` — which needs a `term_id` — cannot be
   seeded at all. The 14/19 split could not ship with the migration.
5. **The proposed privilege split was backwards.** `Unmap`'s doc comment is "End a mapping", yet the
   proposal gated the whole grid save under `Map` and put `Unmap` on exception-delete. As proposed a
   `Map` holder could have ended every mapping in a term.

Only (5) came from reviewing the delta; (1)-(4) came from the pre-dispatch sufficiency check, and
(2) surfaced only because AC-2's arithmetic did not reconcile. **The was/now workaround does not
scale** — it depends on the orchestrator catching each conflict while writing each card.

### Human rulings

Shared subject = one row mapped to both sections. `code` **nullable and unseeded** — decisive
evidence was `18-appendix-c-result-sheet-contract.md:47` declaring `subject_code` *optional* while
`subject_name` is not; nothing prints a code today and the school's own sheets carry none. The
14/19 split ships as `POST /subject-mappings/prefill`, an eighth path. Closure accepted with
`Secret scan` red.

### Two corrections to the ORCHESTRATOR's own work

- **`subject_mapping.copy_same_term` was approved and never existed.** Not in any source file, not
  in the promoted contract. The real mechanism is a FluentValidation rule surfacing as the
  house-wide `request.validation_failed`. `backend-dev` tested the real mechanism and flagged the
  mismatch rather than inventing the code to match the paperwork.
- **The delta archive never transcribed the proposal it approved**, recording amendments and a
  "confirmed as proposed" list pointing at a dispatch message. `STATE.md`'s index advertises that
  file as holding an approved delta *verbatim*. That is why the phantom code survived approval.
  **Rule going forward: transcribe the proposal, then amend it in place — never approve by
  reference to a message.**

### Process

**Four dispatches, two killed by rate limits** (session, then weekly — the 4th and 5th such deaths
on this project). Both times the tree was inventoried before any view was formed of progress; the
second time it looked ~complete and had **zero tests**, with 7 pre-existing tests failing on two
missing `Substitute.For<>` registrations in the unit harness — a test-harness gap, not the DI bug it
resembled. **The `.agent/**` boundary held across all four dispatches**, after failing twice on
earlier cards.

Review found one real gap in the test suite: several rejections asserted `Error.Code` only, leaving
the spec's own sentences unprotected. Now pinned. The suite is not vacuous — every reject-path has a
succeeds-sibling, checked deliberately.

### Left undone, deliberately

`GetArmSubjectsHandler` has no dedicated tests (pure projection over a well-covered resolver).
`CreateSubjectHandler`'s `code_duplicate` path is untested at unit level — flagged by the agent,
adjacent to the review gaps rather than inside them, and now carried as a note on TASK-0072.

  attendance and remarks.

  recompute in Approved after a scale edit. It never changes state.

## 2026-09-17 — TASK-0079 closed: client regenerated against `c5c4d6c6…`, `apiPut` added

Regenerated `frontend/src/api/schema.d.ts` against the committed contract (`sha256` verified
`c5c4d6c6b8d48770fe40f0d2f860f02dc75c818588f2d51ed2dab14a01fe7510`, 64 paths, 132 schemas) and added
`apiPut` to `src/api/client.ts`. Whole-file regeneration: 2,674 insertions / 221 deletions across
`schema.d.ts`, `client.ts` (+25/-1) and `README.md`, plus one new test file.

**The zero-new-wrapper-code streak ended here, exactly where `src/api/README.md` predicted it would.**
Every regeneration since TASK-0029 (0037, 0040, 0044, 0047, 0052, 0074) needed no new transport code.
`SaveScoreSheet` on `PUT /arms/{armId}/score-sheets` is the contract's first PUT with a caller, and the
README had carried a standing prediction since TASK-0029 that this is the point the streak breaks. It
did, and the prediction is now spent — the README lines were updated rather than left to mislead.

**`putRequest` already existed** in `@/lib/http/request.ts` and was already re-exported; it had simply
never been imported into `client.ts`. So the change is a typed wrapper only, no transport work.
`apiPut` is a structural mirror of `apiPatch`: same `splitOptions` → `buildPath` → verb-request shape,
same `SuccessBody`/`RequestBodyOf` generics.

**CSRF needed no interceptor change, and this was verified rather than assumed.** The orchestrator read
`http-client.ts:81` directly: the request interceptor attaches `X-CSRF-Token` on a DENYLIST
(`method !== 'get' && method !== 'head'`), not an allowlist of verbs, so PUT was already covered. Had it
been an allowlist, `apiPut` would have shipped a silent CSRF bypass on every score-sheet save. This is
the check worth repeating on any future new verb.

**Non-vacuity proven TWICE, by two different mutations.** The implementing agent injected a dropped
`Idempotency-Key` thread (RED, then reverted byte-identical). The orchestrator did not accept that on
report and ran an INDEPENDENT mutation per the TASK-0075 precedent — swapping `putRequest` for
`patchRequest` inside `apiPut` — which took the suite to 3 failed / 1 passed, then back to 4/4 on
revert. The test is comparative by construction: it runs `apiPut` and `apiPatch` against two real
contract routes with a shared Idempotency-Key and asserts the CSRF header, the key and the content type
line up while `request.method` differs. It also pins ABSOLUTE values (`'csrf-shared-token'`,
`'shared-idempotency-key'`) rather than only asserting the two sides agree — which is what stops a
both-null vacuous pass, the TASK-0073 failure shape.

Gates, all re-run by the orchestrator, none accepted on report: `check:api-drift` → `No drift`, exit 0
(which also proves `schema.d.ts` carries no hand edit, since regeneration reproduces it byte-for-byte);
`npm run verify` → 55 test files, 381 tests, 0 failed, **Skipped 0**, build clean, exit 0.
`CONTRACT.lock` and the live document both read `c5c4d6c6…`. Nothing outside `frontend/src/api/` was
touched; `openapi-typescript` stayed pinned exact at 7.13.0 and the lockfile is untouched.

**The card deliberately consumed nothing.** `UpdateAssessment`, `UpdateGrading`, `ResetGrading` and now
`SaveScoreSheet` are all callable; none is called from application code. That remains the score-entry
screen card's job.

**Process note:** the dispatch had to resolve two documents that contradict each other. `gates.md` §1
lists `check:api-drift` under what `frontend-dev` does not run, while the card's AC-1 requires it; the
orchestrator ruled the agent may run it as a self-check because it is cheap, with the orchestrator's
re-run authoritative (the TASK-0074 precedent). The `.agent/**` write prohibition was handled by having
the agent emit a `LEDGER ACCOUNT` section for the orchestrator to append — see the drift entry.

## 2026-09-18 — HUMAN RULING: the seeded result rules keep `requireCorePass: true`

Resolves the 2026-09-18 drift entry that a fresh database's default result rules fail their own PUT
validator. The orchestrator recommended, and the human accepted:

1. **Seed unchanged** — `requireCorePass: true`, `coreSubjectIds` empty. Seeding `false` was rejected: it
   would silently weaken promotion for any school that never opens the screen.
2. **Validator unchanged and strict** — core pass required with no core subjects is a contradiction.
   Exempting the untouched seed was rejected as an API special case every client must learn.
3. **The result-rules screen requires core subjects before its first save**, pre-selecting English and
   Mathematics (§6.2.8's stated expected choice) for the administrator to confirm or change. A suggestion
   the admin confirms, never a guess the system saves — preserving TASK-0077's refusal to seed by name.
4. **Third Term publication and promotion (TASK-0036) refuse the incomplete state** with a clear "result
   rules incomplete" rejection, so a school that never opens the screen meets an error, not a silently
   wrong promotion.

The screen makes the default workable; the publication/promotion check makes the incomplete state
impossible to act on unnoticed. Carried into ROADMAP (screen and publication rows) and TASK-0036's card.

## 2026-09-18 — HUMAN RULINGS on TASK-0071: fractional-average banding; 403 for an unknown result set

Both raised by the implementing agent as judgment calls, both recommended by the orchestrator, both accepted.

1. **A fractional average is banded by threshold.** Bands are whole numbers and gapless over whole numbers
   (§6.2.5 rule 6); the average is 2dp (§8.3); the spec says nothing on banding a fraction, and a literal
   closed-interval reading leaves 84.33 in no band at all, which stops computation. Ruled: half-open
   `[lower, upper+1)` — a pupil takes the band whose lower bound they have reached, so 84.60 is B and 85.00
   is A. Same semantics as `passMark` ("at or above"). Rejected: rounding first, which awards A to a pupil who
   never reached 85. The engine already implements this; the §8.4 fixture's averages do not distinguish the
   two readings, so its own unit tests carry the proof. **The school should be told** — it decides printed
   grades — logged as a drift line with trigger "before the first result sheet is printed".
2. **An unknown `resultSetId` on compute is 403, and the contract drops the 404.** The scope layer fails closed
   on `ScopeResolution.Unresolvable` before the handler runs, for every caller; compute is the first route
   whose scope needs a lookup, so it is the first to show it. Fail-closed is kept (safer, and does not reveal
   which ids exist). Changing unresolvable-target semantics would touch every scoped route and belongs to
   TASK-0060. The unreachable 404 is removed from the documented responses before promotion — not breaking,
   the endpoint is unreleased. `result_set.not_found` stays in the handler as defence in depth.

### 2026-09-19 Agents can no longer push; main is PR-only from this machine

**What happened:** on 2026-09-18 an agent (the human confirmed it was an agent, not the editor) pushed `task-0072` to `origin/main`
three times: `b9ee49f` at 21:34, `7ec097d` at 21:35 and `fb32a44` at 21:55. Stages 1-3a landed on main without a PR or CI review.
**Contributing cause (orchestrator's):** the branch was created with `git switch -c task-0072 origin/main`, which set its upstream to
`origin/main`, so a plain `git push` went to main. The upstream was unset on 2026-09-18.

**Controls now in place:**
1. `.claude/agents/backend-dev.md` and `frontend-dev.md`: the agent never pushes, pulls, merges, rebases or touches remotes, and
   never bypasses the hook or the settings.
2. `.claude/settings.json` `deny`: `git push` (bare, with arguments, `git -C … push`) in Bash and PowerShell; `git config
   core.hooksPath`; edits to `.git/hooks/**`. It was proven live when the orchestrator's own dry-run push was refused.
3. `.git/hooks/pre-push` (local, not versioned) refuses any push to `refs/heads/main` or `refs/heads/staging`. Proven: exit 1 for
   main, exit 0 for a feature branch.
4. **Orchestrator's rule:** branch with `git switch -c task-#### --no-track origin/main`, and never leave a feature branch tracking main.

**Still recommended, and the human's to do:** GitHub branch protection on `main` and `staging` (require a PR and passing checks,
no direct pushes). That is the only control a different machine or a `--no-verify` cannot bypass.

### 2026-09-19 TASK-0083 contract delta approved

**Paths.** `GET`/`PUT /api/v1/arms/{armId}/trait-ratings?termId=` (`GetTraitRatings`, `SaveTraitRatings`) and `/development-ratings`
(`GetDevelopmentRatings`, `SaveDevelopmentRatings`). GET needs `result.view`; PUT needs `result.trait.enter` (R3). All are
arm-scoped. The paths depart from §6.7.13's `/result-sets/{id}/traits` on the TASK-0076 precedent: the first save must work
before a result set exists, so the PUT creates or reuses the result set as `SaveScoreSheetHandler` does.

**Behaviour.** The sheet carries an opaque `version`, and a stale save is 409. Rows are in surname order. Trait GET:
`blocks[] { scale, traits[] }`. Development GET: `domains[]`, each with its scale and active indicators, plus `activeIndicatorTotal`.

**Errors.** 409 `result_set_locked` / `stale_version`. 422 `section_not_rated`, `unknown_*`, `*_archived`, `point_not_on_scale`,
`pupil_not_in_arm`, `comment_too_long`. No 404: an unknown arm is 403.

**Sections.** `SectionDto.ratesTraits` is required in the response. It is optional on POST (default false) and PATCH.
The migration sets Primary true and Nursery false.

**R2 refusals.** These are new 409 codes on the three settings PUTs:
`settings.ratingscales.point_rated`, `settings.developmentdomains.scale_changed_while_rated` and
`settings.traits.scale_changed_while_rated`. The wording follows §6.2.13's register.

**Orchestrator amendments.** Promote once after stage 3, because stage 3 moves the settings endpoint descriptions. The trait GET
shape is fixed as blocks.

**Human rulings.**
- Q1-A: an omitted key in a row is left untouched; an explicit null clears (deletes).
- Q2-A: pupils who left during the term are omitted from the entry GET, as on the score sheet.
- Q3-A: a development comment requires a point; clearing the point clears the comment. A comment without a point is 422.

**Stages.** (1) `ratesTraits` + `trait_rating` + trait endpoints + real `ITraitUsageGate`; (2) `development_rating` + endpoints
+ real `IDevelopmentIndicatorUsageGate`; (3) the R2 refusals, then promotion by the orchestrator.

### 2026-09-19 TASK-0086 carded: human rulings L, A, T, H, and the delta approved

Four spec conflicts were put to the human before the card was written. All four were settled as recommended:
- **L:** remarks are capped at 300 characters. That is what C.6, E.5, F and B.11 say. §6.7.7's 240 is superseded, and the fix belongs in the
  next spec revision.
- **A:** `attendance_entry` stores `times_present` only, and absent is derived as `times_school_opened − present` (C.5: "never typed, so
  the three figures cannot disagree"). It is null while opened is blank. The term update refuses to lower opened below the highest stored present.
  This departs from §6.7.7 and `02-data-model.md`, which type both figures.
- **T:** there are two template lists, split by `kind` (ClassTeacher/HeadTeacher). Managing the class-teacher list needs `result.remark.classteacher` at ANY
  scope, and a new any-grant `ScopeResolution` mode exists for it: `PrivilegeDecision` `NotApplicable` requires school-wide, which
  would 403 every arm-scoped class teacher. This departs from §6.7.7's single list.
- **H:** the head teacher's remark is editable in Draft, Returned, Awaiting Approval and Approved. That works under both §6.7.7 (written at approval) and the
  6.7.12 amendment (in the completeness gate); the gate card decides which applies.

**Orchestrator decisions in the delta (additive, approved in the card):** the routes are arm-scoped on the TASK-0083 precedent. `fillEmpty` never
overwrites an existing remark. A duplicate template is 409. Delete is hard. Each sheet has its own version.

`rules/contract.md` §3 permits parallel dispatch only when neither task changes the contract, and TASK-0086 does. The human asked for
0084 to run "alongside if possible". The risk the rule guards against is two agents moving the contract at once. That is excluded here: 0084's
contract impact is none, and its card requires it to STOP on any contract impact. It runs in its own worktree on a branch off
`origin/main`, and each Testcontainers run gets its own Postgres.

### 2026-09-19 TASK-0084 closed: the 401 was a legitimate interleaving, not a product defect

The verdict is H1. Authentication runs once per request, in `CookieSessionAuthenticationHandler.HandleAuthenticateAsync` (`:29-46`), and it runs before routing.
`HttpCurrentUser` only reads the principal that produced. The `FOR UPDATE` lock (`AdminAccountRepository.cs:185-198`) and the session revocation
(`ChangeAdminAccountStatusHandler.cs:117-156`) commit in `UnitOfWork` (`:34-53`), strictly after that request's own authentication. The loser therefore
gets 409 if its request authenticated before the winner committed, and 401 `authentication.session_revoked` if it authenticated after (spec §6.1.13: the next
request from a deactivated session is 401). H2 is ruled out because no re-authentication path exists. H3 is ruled out because the cookie jars are independent and no clock is involved.

The fix is test-only (1 file, +147/-6). There are now two tests. Interleaving (a) holds the same row lock in an external transaction and releases it once `pg_locks` shows
both requests queued. A blocked waiter shows as either a `tuple` lock or a `transactionid` lock, and the predicate covers both. That interleaving asserts 200 + 409. Interleaving (b) sends the requests
sequentially and asserts 200 + 401. Both then assert exactly one active Super Admin. The orchestrator reviewed the diff and accepts the barrier's soundness: session
authentication takes no row locks, so a request waiting on the lock has necessarily authenticated.

Gates: the agent ran unit 997/997 and scoped integration 82/82 three times. The orchestrator ran `ci.ps1 -NoFailFast -IntegrationFilter 'AdminAccount|Session|Auth'` in the worktree:
ALL GATES PASSED, 1112/1112, Skipped 0, on the local container. The commit is `3cfc232` on branch `task-0084`, off `origin/main`. It needs its own PR, is independent of `task-0083`/`task-0086`, and has not been pushed.
The drift entry of 2026-09-16 is struck.

### 2026-09-21 TASK-0088 carded: readiness, completeness gate, submission; two human rulings; delta approved

**Rulings, both the recommended option:**
- **Head teacher's remark gates PUBLICATION only, not submission.** The §6.7.12 amendment adds it to the §6.7.5 gate, but §6.7.7 has
  it written on the approval screen after submission, §6.7.9 lists it as a publication precondition, and ruling H (TASK-0086) keeps
  it editable until Published. Readiness reports it as an informational fifth counter. Resolves TASK-0086 carried-forward item (b).
  Recorded as drift against the amendment (owner: human, next spec revision).
- **The `needs_recompute` triggers are built in the same card, in stage A, before submit.** Seven §6.2.9 settings handlers, five
  subject-mapping handlers, and Returned for Correction→Draft. The transfer trigger (and Awaiting Approval→Draft on a transfer in)
  is deferred, because no code path closes an enrolment (`Enrolment.Close`/`Transfer` exist with no Application caller).

**Orchestrator decisions in the delta:**
- Readiness is arm-routed (`GET /arms/{armId}/readiness?termId=`), departing from §6.7.13's `/result-sets/{id}/readiness`,
  so a Not started arm answers too. This is the same reason the score-sheet routes departed.
- The submit 422 carries `readiness` as a typed problem extension (`ResultSetNotReadyProblemDetails`), the contract's first. The
  generic `errors` map (score-sheet style) cannot carry counts or pupil lists, and §6.7.5 forbids a second call.
- The race drift (present > opened) is closed by definition: attendance counts as complete only when present ≤ opened.
- **A new lock rule, found at carding and not in any drift entry:** `result_set` has no concurrency token, so a settings flag
  committing while compute runs is overwritten by compute's `NeedsRecompute = false`. Every writer of state/flag and every
  state-checking sheet save takes a row lock on the result set, proven by two real-concurrency tests.

- **Orchestrator design, forced by §9.6 and 04 line 77:** Cloudinary `authenticated` delivery, bytes streamed through the API's privilege-checked
  endpoints (a public URL or redirect would lose the privilege check, `nosniff` and `Content-Disposition`); SkiaSharp processing before upload so
  EXIF never leaves the server; assets immutable, because the publication snapshot must keep rendering the logo in force when it was taken.
- Four stages: processor (no contract), store port + entity + upload + fingerprint hash, serving, Cloudinary adapter + runbook. Promote once after the
  contract-moving stages. Drift raised: NDPA processing agreement for a third-party store (owner: human).

### 2026-09-21 TASK-0090 and TASK-0091 closed

- **TASK-0090's delta said approve/return were "scoped `ResultSet` exactly like submit". Wrong: spec 4.4.5 makes both school-wide, and §6.7.8's
  whole point is that the class teacher cannot approve. The route declaration threw against the non-scopable privileges, and the agent flipped
  them to `Scopable: true` — which would have let an arm-scoped grant approve its own arm.** Caught on review of the report's judgement calls, not
  by any gate. Fourth orchestrator delta error (after 0069, 0070, 0077). Lesson: a delta naming a scope must check the privilege's `Scopable` in the
  registry first; and a dev agent editing `PrivilegeRegistry.cs` to make a delta compile is an auth change to STOP on, not a defect to fix.
- Fixed `51d4baa` (school-wide, 404 unknown id, real 403s, scope-metadata guard) and `22ecade` (test data). Integration 14/14 by the orchestrator.
- Promotion `584a4a3c…` → `e7113c87…`: 2 paths + 3 schemas; `ResultSetSummaryDto` gains response-only `returnReason` (in no request body: checked).
- Closing gate ALL PASSED 1384/1384, Skipped 0. TASK-0091: `be208a0`, verify 55/381 on a quiet machine (the 0089 timeout did not recur).
